## Session 4 brief

Scope: the literature for Test A (two-lump interaction potential V(r) against separation, and whether it decays or grows) and Test B (far-field radiation spectrum of the lone oscillon, and whether all emission lies above the mass threshold m) of `SESSION_04_PLAN.md`, and the fourteen predictions on record, A-P1 to A-P7 and B-P1 to B-P7. Date: 2026-11-09. Model claude-sonnet-5-5; a "reasoning_effort" value of 30 is visible in my context (CLAUDE.md lists xhigh for this worker; the mapping is not visible, so I record the mismatch). Tags as above: `READ`, `ABSTRACT`, `SECONDHAND`, `DERIVED`, "no source found". The tools are web search and page fetch: "no source found" means these queries and the pages opened returned nothing, not that nothing exists. How sources were read: as in G to J, the fetch tool saved the PDF of each paper it could not convert as text, and I extracted the text locally with a small PDFKit script (compiled in the session scratchpad); `READ` means I read the passage in that text, and where I say which sections, only those were read. Abstract pages came through the fetch tool's answer. `SECONDHAND` here means a search summary or another paper in the record. No git commands, no simulations, nothing written outside `docs/`. Paraphrase only. Searches 1 to 226 are not repeated: this brief cites the earlier sections by label (S1.5, S6.1 to S6.4, T2, T4, A5, N33, N34, H, J) and adds only what is new. Search numbering continues at 227; `DERIVED` notes continue at N39.

### U0. Headline findings

1. **Every published force or potential I found between two lumps of a massive field, with the lumps below the gap, is exponential in the separation, with decay constant sqrt(m^2 - omega^2).** 3D, conservative cubic-quintic NLS: U = -4 pi abs(A)^2 cos(psi) exp(-kappa R)/R (Malomed 1998, S6.1). 1D complex Q-balls: F proportional to exp(-2 a kappa) (Bowcock et al. 2008). 1D real small-amplitude oscillons: F proportional to exp(-2 a epsilon) (Campos et al. 2026). 1D sine-Gordon breathers: time-averaged V proportional to exp(-sqrt(1 - omega^2) separation) (Kevrekidis, Khare, Saxena 2004). **No source found for a growing (confining) or power-law force between lumps of a single massive real or complex scalar in flat space, in any dimension** (searches 230, 233, 235, 238, 246, 247). U1.3 lists the published long-range cases; each has a cause this model lacks (a massless field, a non-quadratic vacuum, a false vacuum, or an optical radiation channel).
2. **The one thing the literature leaves open for "decays to zero" is radiation.** Optical solitons are bound or pulled together at long range through dispersive radiation (Yulin et al. 2013 `READ`; Smith & Mollenauer 1989 and Akhmediev & Buryak 1995 `SECONDHAND`); no source found for the same between oscillons. `DERIVED` N40: an interference term of the two lumps' 3 omega radiation would fall as 1/r in energy (not exponentially) and alternate in sign with period 2 pi/k3 = 2.48; it would overtake the Yukawa tail somewhere between r of about 24 and 48 depending on the radiated power. The plan's dynamical grid (step 2 for r from 14 to 36) is **coarser than that period**, so a real oscillation of that kind would alias into a smooth wave of period about 10 in r. This is a derived estimate, not a published prediction; it is the main way A-P1 and A-P3 could miss for a reason the literature would call familiar.
3. **The project's position on V_raw is published, and it is not unanimous.** Battye & Sutcliffe 2000 (eq. 3.3 and the text after it) and Bowcock et al. 2008 (section 4) both say that putting a superposed two-Q-ball ansatz into the energy functional does not give an interaction energy, because the charge of the ansatz changes with the separation and the phase. Axenides et al. 2000 nevertheless define the interaction energy by subtracting the single-lump energy densities from the energy density of the superposed ansatz (1D and 2D complex), without comment on the charge. `DERIVED` N39: for the period-averaged functionals V_raw = U + omega times the extra adiabatic invariant, exactly, and the ratio is about omega^2 r/kappa - 1, which is 2r for the primary; it agrees with A-P4.
4. **Gap.** No published claim of emission below m by a single real scalar oscillon in flat space (searches 232, 247). The routes beyond the gap that I found all change the model: a massless (gapless) potential, where the fundamental itself radiates (Dorey et al. 2023 `READ`); a coupled lighter field (Hertzberg 2010, section VI `READ`; Graham 2007 `SECONDHAND`); quantum emission, which Hertzberg finds, for a symmetric potential, as 4 phi to 2 phi at energy 2 omega per quantum (1.82 m for the primary), above m and an even multiple that a classical PDE does not contain. Published far-field spectra of single-field oscillons show lines at integer multiples of the fundamental (2 omega in phi^4, 3 omega for symmetric potentials), **and a wing at and just above omega = m from the width of the fundamental's peak** (Salmi & Hindmarsh 2012; Andersen & Tranberg 2012; both `READ`). B-P3's "no fundamental" conflicts with that; the other clauses of B-P3 agree.
5. **At rate dips the 5 omega line takes over from 3 omega, but the zero is not exact.** The only far-field 3 omega versus 5 omega spectrum I found is Zhang et al. 2020, Fig. 5 (tanh potential; the 5 omega peak is larger than 3 omega at the dip); Ibe et al. 2019 say the decay rate is not exactly zero at the zeros of the 3 omega source because of other modes. The "at least 10 times" numbers in B-P4 have no source; Zhang et al. caution that absolute peak heights in their transforms are not robust.
6. **Corrections to the plan's literature column** are in U5: A-P1 cites Battye & Sutcliffe 2000 for a Yukawa overlap force, which that paper does not give (it warns against the energy-of-ansatz method instead); "confinement would need a massless mediator" is incomplete (a false vacuum also confines, in 1+1D); A-P3's "every primary source agrees on the signs" has the known verbal exception (Campos 2026) and refers to the period average; B-P3's "no fundamental" conflicts with published far-field spectra; A-P6's "k tends to min(kappa_1, kappa_2)" depends on a force convention.

### U1. The interaction between two lumps, against separation

#### U1.1 Published forms and decay constants

| Source | System | Form and decay constant | Tag |
|---|---|---|---|
| Malomed 1998 (S6.1) | 3D conservative cubic-quintic NLS, identical solitons | U = -4 pi abs(A)^2 cos(psi) exp(-kappa R) cos(q R)/R, q = Im kappa (zero when conservative); lowest order in 1/R; R many decay lengths; interaction part of the grand potential at fixed omega | `READ` (S6.1) |
| [Bowcock, Foster, Sutcliffe 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0809.3895), sec. 4 | 1+1 complex Q-balls, general potential | momentum-flux force for equal frequencies, F = -4 cos(theta) A_omega^2 kappa^2 exp(-2 a kappa) (eq. 4.9, a = half the separation), with tail f ~ A_omega exp(-kappa abs(x)), kappa = sqrt(1 - omega^2); negative (attractive) in phase, positive at theta = pi; valid only for exactly in-phase or exactly anti-phase pairs; in the two models studied A_omega = 2 kappa so F is proportional to kappa^4 (eq. 4.10); the same method gives the unequal-frequency result of U1.2 | `READ` |
| [Campos, Manton, Mohammadi 2026](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12633), sec. IV | 1+1 real small-amplitude (Fodor) oscillons about a false vacuum | eqs. IV.10 to IV.12: equal oscillons F = 4 A^2 eps^2 exp(-2 a eps) [eps^2 cos(delta) + cos(2 omega t + delta~)], eps = sqrt(1 - omega^2) in units m = 1 (consistent with the coefficient (1 + eps1 eps2) - omega1 omega2 = 2 eps^2 at equal eps): **the oscillating term is 1/eps^2 times the constant one**; the constant force gives a displacement growing as t^2, the oscillating one a bounded wiggle plus (for sin delta~ not zero) a linear drift | `READ` |
| [Kevrekidis, Khare, Saxena 2004](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0410045) | 1+1 sine-Gordon breathers, Manton's momentum-flux method | instantaneous force proportional to [1 - cos(2 omega t)/(1 - omega^2)] exp(-sqrt(1 - omega^2) separation) (eq. 21); the potential is non-autonomous and its time average is attractive for breathers in phase, V = -(64/omega)(1 - omega^2)^(3/2) exp(-sqrt(1 - omega^2) separation); compared with the PDE only up to t about 35 (Fig. 4) | `READ` |
| [Axenides et al. 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-ph/9910388), sec. III | 1D and 2D complex Q-balls, frequencies omega_plus, omega_minus | interaction energy of the superposed ansatz, from subtracting each lump's energy density: E_int = sigma_plus sigma_minus cos[(omega_plus - omega_minus) t] F + (sigma_plus sigma_minus)^2 cos^2[...] G; because the fields fall exponentially the F term dominates; breathing at the difference frequency with slow drift from the constant term, confirmed by simulation (separation 10, 1D) | `READ` |
| [Manton 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.03557), abstract and introduction | 1+1 real scalar, kinks, octic potential with a quartic minimum | when the vacua are quadratic minima the tails are short-ranged and the force between well-separated kinks is exponentially small in the separation; with a quartic minimum the tail is 1/x and the force is a power law, fourth power of the separation, kink-kink repulsion four times the kink-antikink attraction; the paper also says that any such formula holds only at leading order in the separation and that the centre positions must be carefully defined | `READ` |
| [Christov et al. 2019](https://arxiv.org/abs/1811.07872) | 1+1 phi^(2n+4), n greater than 1 | power-law force, exponent 2n/(n - 1) | `ABSTRACT` |
| [Loiko, Perapechka, Shnir 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.11929), conclusions | 3+1 Friedberg-Lee-Sirlin two-component Q-balls | with the potential switched off the real component is massless, has a Coulomb-like tail and a scalar charge that gives a weak long-range attraction; the complex component gives a short-range Yukawa interaction, attractive in phase and repulsive out of phase | `READ` (conclusions only) |

Reading for the project. (i) The decay constant in every row that has a gap is the tail constant of the lumps, kappa = sqrt(m^2 - omega^2): this is the content behind A-P1's 0.4165 (primary) and 0.2196 (b5). (ii) The 3D law has the 1/R of the Yukawa function; the 1D laws have none; the project's Session 2 law carries the next term (kappa/d + 1/d^2), which Manton warns is the part of such formulas that depends on how the centre is defined. (iii) The rows with a long-range law are all cases where the field around the lump has no gap (massless or quartic vacuum): that is the published meaning of "long-range tail", and it is absent here (single vacuum for beta above 5/6, Session 1 section 1.2). (iv) The oscillating part of the force has amplitude 1/(1 - omega^2) times the mean in the two 1+1D cases (Campos IV.12; Kevrekidis eq. 21): `DERIVED` N44 gives the numbers for the three points.

#### U1.2 Unequal frequencies

- **Axenides et al. 2000** (`READ`, above) and **Bowcock et al. 2008** (`READ`, eq. 4.7): for omega1 not equal to omega2 the leading force is a zero-mean oscillation at the difference frequency. In Bowcock's notation the net momentum flux into an interval [-L, L] between the lumps is cos((omega2 - omega1) t + theta) times 4 A1 A2 exp(-a (kappa1 + kappa2)) (1 + kappa1 kappa2 - omega1 omega2) sinh((kappa2 - kappa1) L), which is zero only if omega1 = omega2. **So with unequal frequencies the interval between the lumps gains and loses momentum, and the two forces need not be equal and opposite at first order** (`DERIVED` N41 for what this does to the decay constant).
- **Campos et al. 2026**, eq. IV.11 (`READ`, 1+1D real): for unequal epsilon the force has a term at the difference frequency, proportional to (1 + eps1 eps2) - omega1 omega2, and one at the sum frequency, proportional to (1 + eps1 eps2) + omega1 omega2, both multiplied by exp(-2 a eps_mean) exp(-X Delta eps), where X is the point at which the momentum flux is evaluated; **the paper says that different choices of X correspond to different conventions for splitting the system**, and that at leading order only the relative-phase dependence and the factor exp(-2 a eps_mean) are the physical content; it picks X* so that the time-averaged tail magnitudes coincide (eq. IV.4) and notes that the centre-of-mass choice differs. No constant term unless Delta omega = 0.
- **Battye & Sutcliffe 2000**, sec. 3.4 (`READ`): for unequal charges the relative phase is not preserved even without interaction; an in-phase pair with omega1 = 1.8, omega2 = 1.5, a = 3 repelled with almost no charge transfer; a boosted pair exchanged charge by an amount that depends on the phase at contact. The paper gives a mechanical model with a rotating relative phase, **not a beating formula**.
- **Second-order mean term:** Axenides' G term (quartic in the overlap) is the only published constant term; `DERIVED` N34 is the project's version. No source found for its sign or size for real lumps, or for any unequal-frequency force in 3D (searches 244, 235).
- Context from T4, not repeated: Al Khawaja 2010 (1D NLS, exact), Buryak et al. 1998 (incoherent attraction of range exp(-2 sqrt(1 + beta) R)/R), Xue et al. 2025 (stochastic outcomes).

#### U1.3 Long-range, power-law, radiation-mediated and confining claims

| Coulomb-like long-range attraction between Q-balls | Loiko et al. 2018 | extra massless real field | none: no massless field | `READ` (conclusions) |

Lett.; journal details not verified here) | dispersive-wave radiation from the solitons | optical analogue | `SECONDHAND` (search 242 summaries) |

Commun. 121, 109, as listed in Yulin et al.) | quartic dispersion; radiationless solitons with oscillating tails | oscillating-tail precedent for minima at discrete separations; the tails there are standing, not outgoing | `SECONDHAND` (search 248; title read in Yulin et al.'s references) |
| Gravity as the main long-range force between oscillons | [Xue et al. 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01597) | self-gravity | not in this model | `SECONDHAND` (search 235 summary; Xue read in J) |

Radiation pressure on a lump by a plane wave pushes it and leaves it intact (Xue et al. 2025; Ciurla et al. 2024; T2, `READ`). Nothing found treats the force between two oscillons through each other's emitted waves (searches 235, 247).

#### U1.4 Measuring V(r) as an energy, and the t = 0 superposition energy

- **Not a binding energy (published).** [Battye & Sutcliffe 2000](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0003252), sec. 3 `READ`: the total charge of the two-Q-ball ansatz is Q = 2 Q_omega + (omega1 + omega2) cos(alpha) times the overlap integral of the two profiles (eq. 3.3); the extra term is exponentially small in the separation but depends on the relative phase, so, strictly, the ansatz cannot be put into the energy functional to find how the energy depends on the phase **or on the separation**, since that compares configurations of different charge; the authors defer the resolution to a companion paper. Bowcock et al. 2008, sec. 4 `READ`: the method used for topological solitons, adding the two profiles and computing the energy against separation, cannot be applied to Q-balls, because the charge of the ansatz varies with the separation and the energy comparison then means nothing; they use the momentum flux instead. Both are complex-field statements; the real-field adiabatic invariant (S6.1, A5) plays the same role (N39).
- **Used as an interaction energy (published).** Axenides et al. 2000 `READ`: E_int from subtracting single-lump energy densities from the superposed ansatz, shown against time (their Fig. 4), with the caveat that it assumes the ansatz keeps its form during the evolution. Manton 2018 `READ`: the static-ansatz energy against separation is the cruder of the available approaches, its derivative gives an estimate of the force, and the ansatz does not satisfy the field equation. So the superposition energy has a published use as an estimate for topological solitons (fixed topological charge, so no charge problem) and a published warning for Q-balls.
- **Fixed-charge treatments.** Malomed 1998 gives the interaction part of the grand potential at fixed omega (S6.1); Cotner 2016 (J1, `READ`) and Amin & Mocz 2019 (S1.5, `READ`) build effective potentials for non-relativistic solitons whose number is exactly conserved. No source found for a measured V(r) for 3D real scalar lumps, at fixed adiabatic invariant or otherwise (searches 230, 236, 239). The Session 2 centroid-force measurement and the Session 4 static quadratures would be the 3D real-field versions; see U6 for wording.

#### U2.1 Which harmonics are emitted, and how they scale

No source found for the amplitude ratio of the 5 omega to the 3 omega radiation of the sextic family (searches 231, 245). The only numbers for a ratio of lines in a far-field spectrum are Zhang's Fig. 5 (read off a log plot; the authors do not trust absolute heights).

#### U2.2 Emission below the mass gap

- **Linear, flat space, single field: none by construction.** For harmonics with n omega below m the radial equation of the quasibreather (Fodor review, eq. 2.199) has only decaying and growing solutions; nothing propagates. The fundamental of every oscillon in the record is below m (Fodor review sec. 2.7.1; Dorey et al. 2023; Salmi & Hindmarsh; Andersen & Tranberg). A decaying line carries no time-averaged flux (N42).
- **Sub-threshold content that exists: bound, not radiated.** Blaschke et al. 2024 (`READ`; 1+1D, quasi-quadratic single-vacuum model with m = 5): a weakly excited oscillon has, besides its fundamental (4.30, that is 0.86 m), a second core-spectrum line at 4.97 (0.994 m), described as almost at the threshold, and the beat between them gives an amplitude modulation at 0.667 (0.13 m). This is a core spectrum; the passages I read show no far-field spectrum of it.
- **Published routes to emission below m all change the model.** (a) A **gapless** potential: [Dorey, Romanczukiewicz, Shnir, Wereszczynski 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.05308) `READ`: oscillons exist in massless deformations of phi^4 (1+1D and radial 3+1D), where the fundamental always lies above the (zero) linear threshold, so they radiate through the first harmonic with a continuous small leakage; they introduce an effective, amplitude-smeared mass threshold and say the usual statement is that a mass gap is a necessary condition for oscillons. For a gapped theory they restate the standard picture: fundamental below m, emission through higher harmonics. (b) A **coupled lighter field**: Hertzberg 2010 sec. VI `READ`: phi to chi chi is open if m is above 2 m_chi; otherwise annihilations. [Graham 2007](https://arxiv.org/abs/0706.4125) (electroweak oscillon) `ABSTRACT`; a summary in search 232 says the massless photon could in principle carry low-frequency radiation yet the oscillon does not decay rapidly that way (`SECONDHAND`). Li, Yamaguchi, Zhang 2025 (abstract, and the equations read in U2.1) couple an external real scalar to the oscillon (parametric resonance, instability bands). (c) **Quantum emission**: Hertzberg `READ`, sec. V (eqs. 49 to 53 and 62): a coherent oscillon decays by annihilation of its quanta into pairs; the resonance condition fixes the emitted quantum's energy at half the energy absorbed. With a cubic or quintic term the leading process is 3 phi to 2 phi (energy 3 omega/2 per quantum); **for a purely symmetric potential (no cubic or quintic term, quartic coupling negative and sextic positive, as here) the leading process is 4 phi to 2 phi, with energy 2 omega per quantum**, k = sqrt(4 omega^2 - m^2), and the rate is proportional to (lambda_4^2 - lambda_6)^2, which vanishes for sine-Gordon and not for the sextic family at beta = 4/3 or 5 (`DERIVED` mapping: sine-Gordon is beta = 1). For omega = 0.907 to 0.976 the quantum line is at 1.81 m to 1.95 m, **above** the threshold, and it is an even multiple, which the classical PDE does not contain. (d) **Expanding backgrounds**: not searched; not this model.
- **What the record supports for B-P5.** A verdict "emission only above threshold" is what every published single-field flat-space result expects; a sub-threshold line carrying flux at r of 30 or more would contradict linear propagation and would have to be an artifact (sponge, box, window leakage) until the doubled-box run says otherwise. No source found for such a line (searches 232, 247).

#### U2.3 Rate dips: what the vanishing 3 omega source leaves

- The leading radiating harmonic's source transform has zeros at special omega (Zhang 2020; Ibe 2019; Li 2025; CGT 2021; S3, A5, N7, N33 here). Next in line is 5 omega: Zhang Fig. 5 shows 5 omega above 3 omega at the dip of the tanh potential; Li 2025 eq. 2.28 has the 5 omega term in the beta = 4/3 model; Ibe 2019 say the rate does not reach zero at the zeros because of other modes.
- CGT 2021: at the exceptional frequency the leading harmonic is confined and the overall radiation amplitude drops by another factor of the geometric-decoupling ratio; aligning several zeros gives further suppression, but the constructions are fine-tuned.
- Zhang 2020 sec. 7.5: for the phi^6 potential the dips are real in both analytics and numerics, but the analytic positions shift against the numerical ones, and including their effective-mass term does not improve this case. Zhang 2020 (A5): the analytic calculation overestimates the rate inside the dips, which prevents accurate lifetimes there.
- Context from a different system: for self-gravitating oscillatons the Fodor review (sec. 3.5) reports that no frequency removes the oscillating tail altogether.
- No source found for a measured far-field 3 omega to 5 omega flux ratio in the sextic family, at or away from a dip.

#### U2.4 Line widths, modulation, sidebands, the fundamental's wing

- Salmi & Hindmarsh (3D phi^4, far field at r = 80, omega_0 about 0.955) find substantial structure rising immediately above threshold, traced to the large width of the fundamental's peak: the part of it above omega = m escapes as free radiation; the signal near omega = 1 is much stronger than in 2D; the dominant channel in energy is still 2 omega_0 because waves barely above threshold carry little energy. Andersen & Tranberg say the same in different words (the fundamental is present far away only through the part of its peak above m, so the width, which reflects the lack of exact periodicity, matters).
- Fodor review sec. 2.10: the envelope of the field carries a low-frequency modulation (a shape mode); it can store a considerable energy and, since it can decay quickly, the energy radiated by it can be much larger than the minimal radiation of a clean oscillon; fine-tuning the initial data can make it very small.
- Blaschke et al. 2024: the modulation is a beat between two core frequencies (U2.2).
- No source found for the positions or heights of sidebands at n omega plus or minus the modulation frequency in a far-field spectrum, or for a formula for the width of the fundamental's peak in terms of the modulation amplitude (search 241). N43 gives the arithmetic.

#### U2.5 Flux against measured loss, and far-field transform methods

- [Fodor review](https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.03340), sec. 2.10, pp. 118 to 122 (`READ`): the period-averaged energy current was measured in the simulation for near-periodic states prepared by fine-tuning the initial data and compared with the analytic small-epsilon formula: 1D phi^4, the numerical radiation is one to two orders of magnitude above the leading-order prediction at the amplitudes where it could be measured, approaching it as epsilon falls; 1D phi^6 (g5 = 1): much closer; 2D phi^6 with an epsilon ln epsilon correction: closer, with a caution about its validity; 2D and 3D sine-Gordon: compared in figures (not read in detail). These compare a measured flux with an analytic one. I did not see a comparison of a measured far-field flux with the measured decrease of the energy inside the lump in the same run.
- Zhang 2020: decay rate measured from the energy inside r_max/2 (their eq. 6.3), independent of the choice of radius when it is of order 10/m; the analytic rate is compared with it and overestimates it in the dips. Salmi & Hindmarsh and Andersen & Tranberg track the energy inside and outside a box and the total (energy conserved until the radiation reaches an absorbing boundary).
- Methods precedent for Test B: Zhang 2020 transform the field at r = 0 and at r_rad = 50/m over a window of 5000/m; Salmi & Hindmarsh and Andersen & Tranberg transform at r = 80 over 5000 time units; Salmi & Hindmarsh built absorbing boundary conditions for the massive field to keep the far field clean (A1 here).
- Early transient: Andersen & Tranberg and Copeland et al. (S2.6) see an initial shedding before the oscillon stage; Zhang 2020 footnote 15: at large amplitude a very-low-momentum mode with frequency (1 + epsilon) m, epsilon small, appears in a transform and is read as free particles produced while the initial data settle. No source found for a measured chirp of the transient at a far probe (search 243); N43 derives it.

### U3. The fourteen predictions against the sources

Verdict key: **agrees** (a source says the same, with its parameters), **partly** (some clauses agree, one conflicts or is unsupported), **disagrees**, **no source** (untested by the literature; the sources named are the nearest). A number from a different potential or dimension is context, as stated in each row.

| Prediction | Verdict | Source and reasoning |
|---|---|---|

No source found for a growing or power-law force between lumps of a single massive field (searches 230, 233, 235, 246, 247). Citation fix in U5 (Battye & Sutcliffe). N40: an interference term of the 3 omega radiation would decay as 1/r in energy, so "Yukawa preferred over every other form out to r_hi" can fail at large r without any growth; the decay-to-zero clause survives |
| A-P2 For r at least 12, V_N and V_F agree with U_tail within 10%; below 12 they fall below it | **no source** | Malomed 1998 is lowest order in 1/R and needs R many decay lengths (kappa r = 5 at r = 12); Manton 2018 says such formulas make sense only at leading order and that the centre must be defined with care. The 10% at r = 12 tests the subleading term (kappa/d + 1/d^2): its relative size is 1/(kappa d), 20% at r = 12 |
| A-P3 In phase attractive, anti-phase repulsive at every resolved r; no minimum for r at least 7 | **agrees** on signs (period-averaged force); **no source** predicts a minimum for identical real lumps | Signs: Malomed 1998, Bowcock 2008 (F negative at theta = 0), Kevrekidis 2004 (time average attractive in phase), Axenides, Battye & Sutcliffe, Session 1 section 4.1. Minima need a non-decaying oscillating tail (q not zero in Malomed's cos(qR)); the only precedent is optical (Akhmediev & Buryak `SECONDHAND`). N40 and N44: the radiative term would alternate in sign with period 2.48; the instantaneous force oscillates at 2 omega with 5.8 times the mean (1+1D) |

IV.11 (1D and 2D only). Battye & Sutcliffe 2000 give a rotating-phase mechanical model, not a beat law. The decay constant of the published unequal force depends on the splitting point (Campos: convention; N41: min, arithmetic or harmonic mean); the relative acceleration contains both exponentials, so the smaller kappa wins only at long range, and a single-exponential fit over 12 to 36 will return something between. The mean term is second order (Axenides G; N34) |

### U4. Derived notes (N39 to N44)

These are my algebra from the plan's equations and from numbers in `READ` sources. They are not literature claims. Re-derive before relying on them; the reviewer should do so independently.

- **N39 (V_raw against U_static; supports A-P4, bears on A-P5).** For period-averaged functionals of phi = Phi cos(omega t + theta) the adiabatic invariant is I = (omega/2) times the integral of Phi^2 (Ibe eq. 23, Session 1 section 1.1) and E = Omega + omega I identically, with Omega = E - omega I the grand potential at fixed omega. For the superposed pair, E_pair - E_1 - E_2 = (Omega_pair - Omega_1 - Omega_2) + omega (I_pair - I_1 - I_2), an identity. The first bracket is Malomed's U = -2 pi A^2 cos(Delta theta) exp(-kappa d)/d at lowest order (S6.1, N13). In the second, I_pair - I_1 - I_2 = omega cos(Delta theta) times the overlap integral of the two profiles, and for Yukawa tails Phi = A exp(-kappa r)/r that integral is 2 pi A^2 exp(-kappa d)/kappa (prolate spheroidal coordinates; kappa d large). So V_raw = 2 pi A^2 cos(Delta theta) exp(-kappa d) (omega^2/kappa - 1/d) and V_raw/U = -(omega^2 d/kappa - 1). The coefficient omega^2/kappa is 1.984 at the primary, 1.946 at the dip, 4.334 at b5; at the primary V_raw/U is about -23 at r = 12 and -58 at r = 30; the sign is positive for in-phase lumps. At fixed I the interaction energy equals U to first order, with a second-order correction of order (Delta I)^2, proportional to exp(-2 kappa d): of relative order exp(-kappa d), 7e-3 at r = 12. For the t = 0, phi_t = 0 slice (the plan's V_raw) the quadratic cross term is the integral of Phi_1 (-laplacian + m^2) Phi_2 = omega^2 times the overlap plus a Yukawa-type piece of order exp(-kappa d)/d, so the leading omega^2 term, which carries the factor d, is the same; the other coefficients differ from the period average (N22, N23).
- **N40 (a possible radiative term in the pair energy; order of magnitude, low confidence).** Each lump emits outgoing waves at 3 omega with wavenumber k3 = sqrt(9 omega^2 - 1) (2.537, 2.529, 2.751 for the primary, dip, b5; N33). The field of lump 1 at lump 2 acts on lump 2's own 3 omega source at first order in its amplitude (N33: the exchange feeds or drains depending on phase). For two identical radiators the interaction of this kind has the size of the mutual radiation reaction: the ratio of the real part of the retarded Green's function at separation d to the imaginary part at zero separation is cos(k3 d)/(k3 d) (3D massive Klein-Gordon at frequency 3 omega, exp(i k3 d)/(4 pi d)), so with P the power one lump radiates, U_rad(d) is of order (P/(3 omega)) cos(k3 d + psi)/(k3 d) and F_rad of order (P/(3 omega))/d, where psi collects phases and a factor cos(3 Delta theta), which equals cos(Delta theta) at Delta theta = 0 and pi. Against the tail, the d cancels in the crossing: d_x(energy) = ln(2 pi A^2 (3 omega) k3/P)/kappa and d_x(force) = ln(2 pi A^2 kappa (3 omega)/P)/kappa. With 2 pi A^2 = 20,200, kappa = 0.4165, E = 2802 and Gamma = P/E from the published beta = 4/3 range (Session 1 brief section 0 item 4: about 1e-7 at dips to 4e-4 at peaks; I also used 1e-5): P = 2.8e-4, 0.03, 1.1 gives d_x(energy) = 48, 37, 28 and d_x(force) = 44, 33, 24. F_rad at r = 40 for P = 0.03 is 2.8e-4, the size of the 1e-4 residual that A3 of the plan sets aside. Each factor of 10 in P moves a crossing by 5.5. This ignores the 5 omega channel and everything about the source geometry that P does not contain, and gives no sign. **Practical consequence:** the period 2 pi/k3 is 2.48; the dynamical grid has step 2 for r from 14 to 36, so a real oscillation of that period appears as a wave of period about 10.4 in r (phase step 5.07 rad, equivalent to -1.21 rad), which a runs test or the plan's "minimum" rule would take for a smooth feature. The static grid (step 0.25, about 10 points per period) resolves it.
- **N41 (decay constant of an unequal-lump force; from Bowcock eq. 4.7 and Campos eqs. IV.3, IV.4).** Between lumps at -a and +a the tails multiply to exp(-a (k1 + k2)) exp((k2 - k1) x), so the force on "the left body" depends on the splitting point X as exp(-a (k1 + k2) - X (k1 - k2)). X = +a (the right lump's position) gives exp(-k1 d), the left lump's tail evaluated at the right lump; X = -a gives exp(-k2 d); X = 0 gives the arithmetic mean; Campos's X* gives the harmonic mean, exp(-2 k1 k2 d/(k1 + k2)) (algebra: the exponent -a(k1 + k2) + a (k1 - k2)^2/(k1 + k2) = -4 a k1 k2/(k1 + k2)). For two free lumps each force is evaluated near its own position: lump 2 feels exp(-k1 d), lump 1 feels exp(-k2 d). The relative acceleration is therefore a sum of two exponentials, and the smaller kappa wins only at large d: for k1 = 0.35 and k2 = 0.42 (an illustration, not project numbers) the ratio of the two terms, exp(-0.07 d), is 0.43 at d = 12 and 0.12 at d = 30, so a single exponential fitted over 12 to 36 returns a value between the two. Bowcock's momentum is not conserved in the middle interval for unequal frequencies (U1.2), which is why the splitting point matters.
- **N42 (a sub-threshold line far from the lump).** In the linear far field a line at omega_s below 1 obeys (laplacian - (1 - omega_s^2)) phi = 0 with a decaying solution exp(-kappa_s r)/r, kappa_s = sqrt(1 - omega_s^2). Its time-averaged radial flux is zero (the field is a real profile times a single cosine in time, so phi_t and phi_r are in quadrature). For the plan's margin of 3 resolution bins (0.0094) below threshold, omega_s = 0.9906, kappa_s = 0.137, decay length 7.3, and the local log-slope of the amplitude between r = 30 and 60 is -6.9, steeper than the -1.3 criterion; a line closer than that to threshold is excluded from the verdict by the margin. A sub-threshold line that carries "the missing flux" (clause 2 of the plan's "sub-threshold emission present") is self-contradictory in linear theory; it would indicate that the probe region is not linear, that the absorber leaks, or that the window leaks.
- **N43 (chirp, gap and sidebands).** A free Klein-Gordon packet seen at r, t has local frequency fixed by the group velocity r/t = k/omega, so omega_loc = 1/sqrt(1 - (r/t)^2) = t/sqrt(t^2 - r^2) (m = 1): 1.048 at r = 30, t = 100 and 1.005 at t = 300; at fixed r it approaches 1 from above. This is the plan's B-P7 formula; the derivation is standard, and I found no source to cite for it. Gaps 1 - omega are 0.0909 (primary), 0.0935 (dip) and 0.0244 (b5). The published far-field wing above threshold (Salmi & Hindmarsh; Andersen & Tranberg) arises at gaps of 0.036 to 0.045 for phi^4 at omega_0 = 0.955 to 0.964. An amplitude modulation at frequency Omega puts sidebands at omega plus or minus Omega, and the upper one is above threshold when Omega exceeds the gap: that is 0.09 for the first two points and only 0.024 for b5. So b5 is the point at which a near-threshold wing is most likely, and the primary and dip the least; the fundamental's own width (radiative decay and drift of omega over the window) works the same way. No source gives shape-mode frequencies or modulation depths for the sextic family.
- **N44 (instantaneous against mean force).** In the 1+1D results the instantaneous force is the mean times [1 - cos(2 omega t)/(1 - omega^2)] (Kevrekidis eq. 21) or the mean plus an oscillating term 1/kappa^2 times larger (Campos IV.12). 1/(1 - omega^2) is 5.77 at the primary, 5.61 at the dip and 20.7 at b5. The 3D factor is not published; a momentum-flux argument of the same structure is expected to give a similar one. It matters for the window: the measured acceleration must be averaged over whole periods (T = 2 pi/omega = 6.9, 6.9 and 6.4), and the oscillating part of each lump's centroid motion is of order F_osc/(M (2 omega)^2), and should be averaged out by whole periods, not fitted as noise.

### U5. Corrections to the plan's literature column, and to earlier briefs

1. **A-P1, "Battye & Sutcliffe 2000, Q-balls" as a source for a Yukawa overlap force.** I read their section 3 (`READ`): it gives no force law; it says the superposed ansatz cannot be used to find how the energy depends on the relative phase or on the separation, because the charge changes, and defers the resolution. The sources for the force are Bowcock et al. 2008 (1D, with the eq. 4.9 force), Malomed 1998 (3D NLS), Campos et al. 2026 (1+1D real) and the Session 2 derivation. Battye & Sutcliffe support the signs and the qualitative picture only.
2. **A-P1, "confinement would need a massless mediator."** Incomplete. The published mechanisms are (i) massless fields, which give long-range laws (Loiko et al. 2018; Manton 2018 for power-law tails), and (ii) a false vacuum, which binds a kink-antikink pair at a finite separation (Manton 2018 introduction; Rutkevich 2024 abstract), in 1+1D. The second needs a second, non-degenerate vacuum: for this potential one exists only for beta at most 5/6 (Session 1 section 1.2), not at 4/3 or 5.
3. **A-P3, "every primary source agrees on the signs."** One verbal exception (Campos 2026, the sentence after eq. IV.13; its formula agrees; Session 1 section 4.2). The signs refer to the period-averaged force: the instantaneous force changes sign within a period (N44, 1+1D).
4. **A-P6, "Axenides 2000 and Battye & Sutcliffe 2000 (beating of unequal frequencies)."** Axenides states and tests the breathing at the frequency difference; Battye & Sutcliffe give a phase-rotation mechanical model and numerical outcomes, not a beat law. Add Bowcock 2008 eq. 4.7 and Campos 2026 eq. IV.11. And "k tends to min(kappa_1, kappa_2)" depends on the force convention (N41): true for the sum of exponentials at long range, not for a single published expression.
5. **B-P3, "no fundamental."** Published far-field spectra of phi^4 oscillons show power at omega = m and just above it, from the fundamental's width (U2.4). The clause should be "no line at the fundamental; a wing above threshold scaling with the line width may be present". The citation "Fodor et al. 2009" is for 1D and 2D (sine-Gordon in 2D and 3D, phi^6 in 2D); Hertzberg 2010 eq. 18 gives the clean n = 3 statement, and Zhang 2020 Fig. 5 the only far-field 3 omega and 5 omega transform I found (tanh potential).
6. **B-P4, "N33" and "Zhang et al. 2020 and Li et al. 2025 on radiation-rate dips."** Right for the existence of dips and the 5 omega term. Neither gives a flux ratio for the sextic family. Zhang sec. 7.5: for phi^6 the analytic dip positions are off against numerics.
7. **B-P7.** The local-frequency formula is derived (N43), not published.
8. **Earlier briefs.** Session 1 section 4.2 ("at fixed N an energy comparison is not meaningful ... Bowcock et al.") is confirmed, and Battye & Sutcliffe 2000 eq. 3.3 is a second source for it. S1.5's citation of Nishida et al. 2009 (sine-Gordon breather pairs, `ABSTRACT`) was not found again in search 240; the breather-pair law is now `READ` in Kevrekidis, Khare, Saxena 2004. N34 (second-order mean force of unequal lumps) is supported in form by Axenides' G term; no sign or size is published.

### V3. Derived notes (N45 to N49)

My arithmetic. Not literature claims; re-derive before relying on them.

- **N45 (geometric decoupling, from the measured line amplitudes).** With r times a for n omega taken as proportional to gamma^n (CGT eq. 11), -ln gamma = -ln(ratio)/2 for successive odd harmonics, and G = (-ln gamma) kappa/omega. Primary (r = 60 probe): epoch 0 omega 0.9100, kappa 0.4145: 3 to 5 G = 0.957, 5 to 7 0.967, 7 to 9 1.29; epoch 1 (0.9126): 0.968, 0.908, 1.03, 0.953, 0.872 (3 to 5 up to 11 to 13); epoch 2 (0.9198): 0.867, 0.817, 0.816; epoch 3 (0.9521, kappa 0.306): 0.660, 0.634. Dip (0.9068, kappa 0.4215): 5 to 7 1.02, 7 to 9 0.98, 9 to 11 0.92, 11 to 13 0.93, 13 to 15 0.93; 3 to 5 is 0.20 to 0.21 (the confined 3 omega). So G is of order one with a slow drift, and the geometric progression is clean at the dip from 9 omega up (ratio 0.0185 to 0.0188). The exponent per harmonic step -ln gamma is 1.9 to 2.3 in every case (leaving out the 9 omega step at the primary start, 2.8, and the confined 3 to 5 step at the dip, 0.43), flat in omega where a constant G would raise it by about 45% between omega 0.906 (omega/kappa 2.15) and 0.952 (3.12). The core spectrum falls as exp(-b omega) with amplitude slope 0.94 per unit omega for n = 3 to 15 (fit, both points; 1.88 if it were a power): S&H Fig. 13 give b = 1.8 for 3D phi^4 on |Phi(omega)| (axis label; whether amplitude or power not stated in the text I read), so a match only if their axis is power; not decided, context.
- **N46 (the modulation frequency).** nu = 0.0952 from four sidebands (spacing the same at 3 omega and 9 omega, so these are sidebands of the modulated lines and not separate modes), period 66.0 = 9.5 fundamental periods, nu/omega = 0.105. m - omega_core = 0.0932. Resolution 0.0031, so nu - (m - omega) = 0.0020 is 0.6 of a bin. The partner line of the fundamental, omega + nu = 1.0020, falls in the first bin above threshold and cannot be told from the near-threshold band. If nu = m - omega, the modulation is a beat between the fundamental and a mode at the continuum edge, as in Blaschke 2024 (0.994 m, just bound) and as the Fodor 2006 trend (lowest modulation at highest omega) suggests; if nu is below m - omega the mode is bound. Not separable here. Sidebands: 0.35% of the 3 omega line in the far field at epoch 1, about 1e-5 of it at the core (scholar); the primary shows none, which may only mean its large drift (0.0009 to 0.014 per epoch) hides them. The project's start (single-frequency profile, phi_t = 0) is not a quasibreather, so a weakly excited shape mode is expected (Fodor review pp. 52 to 53: minimal shape modes need quasibreather starts).
- **N47 (three small readings).** (a) Fold kinematics. On the stable branch N falls as omega rises and N(omega) has its minimum at omega_death (Session 1, Li 2025 and Ibe 2019 definitions, `READ`). For a steady loss of N, d omega/dt = (dN/dt)/(dN/d omega) grows without bound as omega approaches omega_death; so an accelerating drift in the last epochs is what the family shape gives, and "slowly" cannot hold up to death. The size was not computed: it needs the N(omega) table of Session 1 and the loss rate (cheap, D-B3). (b) Retardation. The line at r = 120 left the lump about 97 time units before the one at r = 30 (group velocity 0.93); the 3 omega r times a changes by +64% (epoch 0 to 1) and +83% (1 to 2) per 5000, i.e. 1.0e-4 and 1.2e-4 per time unit, expecting -1.0% and -1.2% from r = 30 to 120; measured -0.8% and -1.1%; epochs 2 and 3: -0.7% and +1.7% against a changing sign of the trend (rise then fall). Same sign and size in epochs 0 and 1, rough in 2 and 3. The same lag bears on the harmonic-line flux over the lump's loss rate, which is lower at r = 120 than at r = 30 while the loss rate rises and higher while it falls. Expected (relative rate of change of the loss times 97): -1.9%, -2.4%, about 0, +1.0% in epochs 0 to 3; seen from r = 30 to 120: -1.6%, -2.3%, -1.5%, +3.3%. Same sign in all four, size close in epochs 0 and 1, rough in 2 and 3. (c) The first window of the primary reads 0.90965, +0.0005 above the profile's 0.909149; the epoch Welch peak 0.91005 is +0.00090 because it includes the drift. The dip settles up by +0.00033 (0.906836 against 0.9065045), and Session 2 found a settled omega of 0.90689 to 0.90707 from 0.906505 (Addendum F item 2): the start sheds a little N and the settled lump has slightly higher omega.
- **N48 (what the closure can and cannot show).** Energy conservation of the semi-discrete system plus the sponge makes flux(r) = -dE_in(r)/dt exactly in the time average; the numbers in row 11 (0.99995 to 1.00013) are that identity seen through the Hann-squared weights. The test of physics is in the split: sub-threshold flux over the loss at r at least 30 is below 1e-6 in the primary, and the flux in the n omega bands is 0.971 to 1.003 of the loss at r = 30 to 120 against a total of 1.000.
- **N49 (the plan's own evanescent floor).** B3 gives the fundamental's bound tail as 7e-6 at r = 30 and 1e-11 at r = 60 for the primary; the measured line amplitude is 7.50e-6 at r = 30 and 1.0e-8 at r = 45 (epoch 0), 6.35e-9 at r = 60 (epoch 3, kappa 0.306). So B3 predicted what B-P3's last clause denied; the two sat in the same section.

### V5. Proposed checks, not run

- **D-B1** (calculation, no PDE): evaluate Li eq. 2.28 (or Zhang eq. 5.1) from the stored profiles at omega 0.9100 and 0.9068 and compare the 3 omega and 5 omega amplitudes with 0.0167 and 2.49e-4 (primary), 3.74e-4 and 1.575e-4 (dip). The 5 omega term is independent of the 3 omega zero, so it is the cleaner test. The same transform against omega locates the zero of the 3 omega source and says how far 0.9068 is from it.
- **D-B2**: nu against m - omega at a second long-lived beta = 4/3 start with a different gap (Li Fig. 3 has other platforms, at lambda E/m about 88 and 188, at lower omega and larger gap). If nu = m - omega, a lump at omega near 0.88 would show sidebands near 0.12 (period about 52), and one near omega 0.95 sidebands near 0.05 (the primary's last epoch passes through that range but drifts 0.014 per epoch and shows none); if nu is a fixed property of the shape, it would not move. The 2000-unit windows resolve both.
- **D-B3**: dN/dt from the loss rate divided by omega, d omega/dt = (dN/dt)/(dN/d omega) from the Session 1 N(omega) table, against the measured steps 0.0026, 0.0072, 0.0323.

- Honda & Choptuik 2002 and Honda's dissertation (abstract pages only; no shape-mode frequency seen); Blaschke et al. 2025 and 2026 and 2607.15624 (abstracts or summaries); the black-hole and flat-space tail papers of search 255 (summaries only); Li 2025 Fig. 3 and Ibe 2019 Fig. 6 are read from plots (log axes), so the death times quoted are good to a factor of about 2 for Ibe and about 10% for Li.
- No source found for: a measured omega(t) of the beta = 4/3 lump through its last epochs; the far-field 5 omega to 3 omega ratio of the sextic family; a shape-mode frequency of a sextic oscillon; sidebands on radiation lines; a measured flux against measured loss closure in one run; a probe-series measurement of the bound tail; a measured chirp of the formation transient.

## Session 4, Test A against the literature (2026-11-10, after the production runs and the orchestrator's diagnostics)

Scope: the Test A measurements (production of 118 jobs, `analysis.json` of s4-testA de519e4; the orchestrator's diagnostics `diag_production.json`, code 71c7d9e, in worktree agent-a6c4107736c741b0a) against the Session 4 brief (U0 to U8), the Test B section (V1 to V6) and the sources below. Tags as before: `READ`, `ABSTRACT`, `SECONDHAND`, `DERIVED`, "no source found". Model claude-sonnet-5-5; a "reasoning_effort" value of 30 is visible (CLAUDE.md lists xhigh; mismatch recorded again). Nothing written outside `docs/`, no git commands, no simulations. How read: the fetch tool saved the PDF of each paper it could not convert, and I extracted the text locally with a small PDFKit program (scratchpad); `READ` means I read the passage in that text. New this round and read that way: Bowcock, Foster, Sutcliffe sec. 4 (eqs. 4.4 to 4.7 and the two paragraphs after), Siemonsen & East sec. II B, Forbes, Bradshaw, Andrews pp. 3 to 4. Ficek & Tanas Eqs. 32, 33, 39 to 41 were read from the ar5iv HTML through the fetch tool's answer (first 100,000 characters only), so `READ` with that route stated. Numbers marked "(scholar)" are arithmetic on `analysis.json` or `diag_production.json`, not reviewed; the scholar scripts are in the session scratchpad, not in the repo. Claim numbers continue from V1 (16 onward), searches from 257, `DERIVED` notes from N50. Revised the same day after the reviewer's independent check of the diagnostics; where the reviewer's reading differs from the numerics' or from my first draft, the reviewer's stands and the change is marked in the text (W0 items 1 to 3, claims 17 to 23, 27, N50, N52 to N54).

### W0. Headline findings

1. **The measured equal-pair force agrees with every published statement I found; a +3.4% scale over the Yukawa law is not explained, and the formal verdict "decays to zero" is marginal.** Signs (in phase attracts, anti-phase repels, no minimum), exponential decay with kappa = sqrt(m^2 - omega^2), no confinement, no power law: Malomed 1998 (3D), Bowcock et al. (1D), Campos et al. 2026 (1+1D real), Kevrekidis et al. 2004 (breathers), Siemonsen & East 2023 (statement of the same laws). The 1.02 to 1.06 excess over the Yukawa law at r 22 to 28 is, by the orchestrator's fit, a +3.4% +- 0.4% scale. My first reading, that the settled pair's kappa (0.41555 to 0.41570 against 0.41647 for the static profile) accounts for it, was wrong, and the reviewer's check overrides it: kappa alone predicts +2.6% to +3.7% over r 28 to 40, but the tail amplitude A falls with omega and takes 1.6% to 1.9% off the force (A squared), so kappa and A together predict only +0.6% to +1.9% (N54). The +3.4% is an unexplained scale, and at r 26 to 30 the residual is mostly this scale. The settle-up of omega by +4.2e-4 is itself the one N47(c) had recorded (+3e-4 to +5e-4). The verdict "decays to zero" for the primary is accepted by the reviewer but marginal: the clause of a 100-fold fall rests on r >= 34 (without r 34 to 40 both A3 and A3' return undetermined); the evidence is the force table (0.8 at r = 16 down to 1e-4) and a bound on a constant force of about 3e-4 or less, under 0.04% of F(16). E2 is not preferred (Delta AIC 4.6 in favour of E1) and k_E2 = 0.4173 +- 0.0022.
2. **The far-tail residual (r 28 to 40, about 1e-4) is consistent with a first-order interference of the lump with its partner's 3 omega wave and partly confirmed as such (the reviewer's status), and no source found describes it for oscillons, breathers or kinks.** I searched for radiation-mediated, 3 omega-mediated and 1/r oscillating forces between oscillons, breathers, Q-balls and kinks (searches 257, 258, 264, 267, 268, 274, 275, 276): nothing. Published analogues exist in optics and atomic physics, where two coherent radiators have an interaction energy proportional to cos(kR)/R and a collective decay rate proportional to sin(kR)/(kR): Forbes et al. (optical binding, `READ`), Ficek & Tanas Eqs. 32, 33, 39 (two atoms, `READ`), Yulin et al. 2013 and Sakaguchi et al. 2018 (optical solitons, `READ` earlier). My own derivation (N50), the orchestrator's D3 (collective-coordinate, first order) and the reviewer's independent re-derivation give the same form, sign and factors, B0 = k3 Q^2/(8 pi) = P/(3 omega), P the lump's radiated power; with Test B's P = 0.0122 it is 4.45e-3, with the Born Q 3.85e-3, and the fit to the data gives 3.25e-3 +- 0.29e-3 (0.73 and 0.84 of them). Limits on the reading (reviewer and mine): it is not parameter-free (the Born amplitude with delta = 0 gives chi2 42.6 on the 8 points at r >= 32; the free fit is 16% below Born with a phase offset of 0.5 to 0.6 rad, zero in the formula); the step-2 grid cannot tell k3 = 2.54 from its alias pi - k3 = 0.60 (global minimum of the free-k scan at 0.596 with chi2 0.45, the local one nearest k3 at 2.541 with 2.2), so 3 omega is chosen by prior and elimination (N50: the alias would be a wave of frequency 1.17, where Test B sees no line; the 5 omega force is 4e-4 of the 3 omega one; no even harmonics); at r 26 to 30 the residual is mostly the unexplained scale, so the 3 omega reading rests on r >= 32; and chi2_red 0.20 means the quoted sigma_F is about 2.2 times conservative.
3. **The unequal-pair centroid amplitude (0.42 to 0.62 of the static law) is a biased lower bound; on the cleaner estimator the deviation is -7% at r = 18 and beyond 15% from r = 22, and the literature said the leading-order unequal-frequency force is convention-dependent and not reciprocal.** Bowcock et al. eq. 4.7 (`READ`): for distinct frequencies there is a net momentum flux into an interval between the lumps (zero only if omega_1 = omega_2); Campos et al. eq. IV.11 (`READ` earlier): different splitting points are different conventions; Manton 2018 (`READ` earlier): centres must be defined with care. The reviewer finds the centroid estimator contaminated by the beat-phased interference energy of the overlapping tails crossing the mid-plane (the right order at r 22 to 28), and the ball-momentum route reciprocal (F_1/F_2 0.97 to 1.00) for r <= 24, where it gives 0.93, 0.89, 0.84, 0.79 of the static law at r = 18, 20, 22, 24; it falls to 0.74 at r = 26 and 0.47 at 36, where the forces on the two lumps split in the direction Bowcock's structure gives. The reviewer rejects the numerics' inverse-mass re-weighting as the explanation (a single-potential Hamiltonian, which the point model is, has equal and opposite forces; the re-weighted numbers in claim 23 are kept as the numerics' reading and are not relied on). No source quantifies the size of such a coordinate contamination for 3D real lumps. My pre-diagnostic hypothesis (non-reciprocity alone accounts for the centroid number) was not supported as stated (N52).
4. **V_N floor at r about 22 (A-P7).** No published energy-based potential between radiating lumps exists to compare with (searches 270, 274). My estimate (N51): the interference energy of the two lumps' 3 omega clouds over the box falls as 1/(k3 d) and scales with the cloud's extent, not with the lumps' coupling; the local cloud-times-core coupling is three orders smaller. My arithmetic is 5 to 9 times below the offset the orchestrator reports (0.07 to 0.09 at r = 20), so it supports the attribution in kind and not in size.
5. **A correction to my earlier briefs** (W6): arXiv:2001.07476, recorded as "Hertzberg, Schiappacasse, Yanagida" in the Amin & Mou rows, opens to a paper on axion-star nucleation (abstract: no mergers or phases). The Hertzberg paper that does study the phase dependence of mergers is [Hertzberg, Li, Schiappacasse 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.02405) (`ABSTRACT`).

Optical binding: the interaction energy of two particles in a coherent field carries cos(k.R), and in the fully retarded far field it falls as 1/R, not like the 1/R^3 or 1/R^7 of the static and dispersion forces; both oscillating curves are damped with separation ([Forbes, Bradshaw, Andrews, optical binding of nanoparticles](https://ueaeprints.uea.ac.uk/73313/3/Optical_binding_of_nanoparticles.pdf), pp. 3 to 4, `READ`; a driven case, not self-radiating). Two atoms: collective damping Gamma_12 and dipole-dipole shift Omega_12 are sums of terms in sin(k r)/(k r) and cos(k r)/(k r) (and higher inverse powers), both small for r much larger than the wavelength ([Ficek & Tanas 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/quant-ph/0302082), Eqs. 32, 33, 39 to 41, `READ` via ar5iv). Optical solitons: radiation trapped between them gives attraction, with the mechanism "not clearly understood" at large separation ([Yulin et al. 2013](https://arxiv.org/abs/1305.5909), `READ`); oscillating-tail potentials with minima at fixed separations ([Sakaguchi, Skryabin, Malomed 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.02190), Eqs. 5, 6, `READ` earlier; damped exponentially, not 1/R). The 1/r oscillating term here belongs to the first and second group: propagating radiation between coherent emitters |

| A-P7 V_N floor at r = 28 to 34 | **no source** | Claim 28, N51; measured near 22. Ledger F-036 |

Where the plan's predictions conflict with published results: none in kind. The plan's A-P6 clause "k tends to kappa_min" and A-P1's "Yukawa preferred" rest on conventions (U5 item 4, N41) and on a rule, not on a source. A-P2's 10% band had no source and fails on its own terms for r >= 34.

### W3. Derived notes (N50 to N55)

My arithmetic. Not literature claims; re-derive before relying on them.

- **N50 (the coupling to the partner's 3 omega wave; amplitude, aliasing, symmetry, discriminators).** For (box + m^2) phi = S with S = Re[s exp(-i Omega t)], q = integral of s, the outgoing field is Re[q exp(i (k r - Omega t))/(4 pi r)], k = sqrt(Omega^2 - 1) (m = 1), and the flux through a sphere is P = Omega k q^2/(8 pi) (this is 2 pi a^2 Omega k with a = q/(4 pi), the Test B form). Two identical coherent sources at separation d have the time-averaged coupling energy P cos(k d + Delta)/(Omega k d) and, differentiating, a force of amplitude P/(Omega d) at leading order in 1/(k d). With Omega = 3 omega, k = k3 = 2.5375 (omega = 0.909149; wavelength 2.476) and P = 0.0121 to 0.0122 (Test B, epoch 0, r a3 = 0.0167): B0 = P/(3 omega) = 4.47e-3, so B0/d = 1.31e-4, 1.24e-4, 1.11e-4 at r = 34, 36, 40. The orchestrator's D3 gives the same B0 = k3 Q^2/(8 pi) with Q = 4 pi r a3, and the reviewer re-derived the form, sign and factors independently and agreed. **Crossing:** B0/d equals the Yukawa force at d = 34.7 (the formula of N40 with this P), which is where the data show the residual comparable to the law (0.43 of it at r = 34, 0.82 at 36, 6.2 times at 40). **Pre-diagnostic fit (scholar, r >= 30, k3 fixed, free phase, no baseline):** B = 3.27e-3 (3.13e-3 and 3.38e-3 in the two channels), phase 0.67 rad, chi2 2.2 for 9 points; the orchestrator's M6 with the baseline over r >= 26 gives 3.25e-3 +- 0.29e-3 and 0.505 rad. **Aliasing:** sampling at step 2 maps k to pi - k, 2.538 to 0.603: the free-k scan of the orchestrator has its global minimum at 0.596 (chi2 0.45) and a local one at 2.541 (2.19); mine (r >= 30, amplitude 1/r) has minima at 0.655, 2.49, 3.795, 5.63, 6.94 with chi2 1.68 to 1.75; amplitude falling as 1/r or 1/r^2 fits equally (chi2 2.2 and 1.0 at fixed k3, B = 3.3e-3 and 0.117). So the data fix the size and sign pattern of the residual, and the wavenumber only through the lump's own omega, that is by prior and elimination. **Elimination (scholar, from Test B):** the alias k = 0.60 would be a wave of frequency sqrt(1 + 0.36) = 1.17, 1.28 omega, where Test B finds no line (lines only at n omega and in the band 1.0004 to 1.0077); the force of an n omega line scales as k_n (r a_n)^2, so the 5 omega line gives (4.434/2.5375) x (2.49e-4/0.0167)^2 = 3.9e-4 of the 3 omega force; there are no even harmonics (V1 rows 3, 7, 13). **Symmetry:** any term bilinear in the two lumps' fields changes sign when one lump's field is negated (the field sign flip), so a residual proportional to cos(dtheta) at 0 and 180 follows for the Yukawa term, the 3 omega term and a reflected partner wave alike; it excludes only terms even in the partner's field (self-reflection, additive artefacts). The sign-normalised residuals of the two channels agree to 0.4e-5 or better at r >= 28 because the runs are mirror copies, not independent samples. The one test that separates the 3 omega phase from the fundamental's: dtheta = 60 degrees, where 3 dtheta = 180 degrees gives sin(k3 r - pi) = -sin(k3 r) against the Yukawa term's cos 60 = +0.5. **Radiation pressure** (second order in the partner's amplitude, no dependence on the relative phase; Ciurla et al. 2024 sec. 4, `READ` in T2, for the 1D Q-ball) is about sigma Omega/(4 pi d) of B0/d: 0.19 at d = 40 for a cross-section sigma = 35 (geometric, assumed); it cannot supply a residual that changes sign between channels. **Dip lump:** P = 6.0e-6 (Test B), so P/(3 omega d) = 5.5e-8 at d = 40, 2e-3 of the primary's: a dip pair should show no residual at r 34 to 40, whether the radiation acts directly or after a reflection. **Open:** the 0.5 to 0.6 rad offset and the 16% amplitude deficit (the Born amplitude with delta = 0 gives chi2 42.6 on the 8 points at r >= 32, reviewer); a candidate (derived, unchecked) is the s-wave phase shift of a k3 wave on the lump's effective potential, which the Born Q ignores (Test B D-B1 found the Born 3 omega amplitude 0.93 of the measured at the primary).
- **N51 (cloud interference energy and the V_N floor).** Two coherent 3 omega clouds of equal amplitude have, integrated over a large sphere, a cross energy of 2 E_cloud sin(k3 d + psi)/(k3 d) (the average of cos(k3 d cos theta) over angle is sin(k3 d)/(k3 d)). E_cloud is the energy stored in the cloud inside the box, about P L/v_g with v_g = k3/(3 omega) = 0.930 and L the distance to the faces: 0.39 to 0.59 for L = 30 to 45 and P = 0.0122. Counted as E - omega_0 N a cloud quantum carries 3 omega, so V_N sees 2/3 of it: 0.010 to 0.016 at d = 20, falling as 1/d with a sign pattern cos(3 dtheta) and a period 2.476 that the step-2 grid aliases to 10.4. The reported offset is 0.07 to 0.09 (orchestrator): 5 to 9 times larger, so the attribution holds in kind and the estimate misses in size (L, P and the snapshot's cloud amplitude were not checked). The local coupling, the energy form of N50, is P/(Omega k d) = 8.8e-5 at d = 20, three orders smaller. Two predictions that separate this from a noise floor: the offset scales with the box size L at fixed d, and it vanishes for dip pairs (P/2007).
- **N52 (the unequal pair: what the diagnostics say, and what my earlier hypothesis did not).** (a) The orchestrator's finding rewritten in my numbers: the beat-frequency relative acceleration at r = 26 is F_amp (1/E_1 + 1/E_2) = 5.79e-3 x 4.68e-4 = 2.7e-6, and a coordinate dressing c cos(dphi) adds c Delta omega^2 cos(dphi) to it, Delta omega = 0.0484, Delta omega^2 = 2.3e-3: a dressing of c = 1.2e-3 (3.5e-4 of the half-width 3.3) gives the whole amplitude at r = 26 and c = 1.1e-5 at r = 36. At a constant beat frequency it is not separable from a force; for equal lumps there is no beat and no dressing. The reviewer identifies the origin as the beat-phased interference energy of the overlapping tails crossing the mid-plane, of the right order at r 22 to 28. (b) My arithmetic before the diagnostics, not recorded earlier: if the true force on lump 2 were the whole first-order tail term and the force on lump 1 negligible at large r (Bowcock eq. 4.7 and N41 structure), a reduced-mass conversion would give E_1/(E_1 + E_2) = 0.276 of it, or 0.568 if the point model's weight w_2 = 0.486 were also taken out; the plateau 0.42 to 0.44 sits inside [0.28, 0.57]. The diagnostics do not support non-reciprocity as the explanation of the centroid number: the momentum-route forces are reciprocal to 2% at r <= 22 and the momentum amplitude is not a plateau (0.93, 0.74, 0.47 of the static at r 18, 26, 36). They support a reading in which the force on each lump separates at long range in the direction of N41 (momentum F_1/F_2 0.91 at r = 26, 0.39 at 36, against the point model's 0.157 and 0.062), more slowly than first-order theory gives. (c) The measured ratio of the per-lump centroid forces (0.154 at r = 18, falling to -0.03) is not a force ratio. (d) The numerics' inverse-mass re-weighting is rejected by the reviewer: a single-potential Hamiltonian has equal and opposite forces, so the point model's weights omega_i/(omega_1 + omega_2) are part of its law and the pair of weights (0.514, 0.486) against (0.724, 0.276) is not a normalisation error; the cleaner comparison is the momentum amplitude against the point-model static law (0.93, 0.89, 0.84, 0.79 at r 18 to 24).
- **N53 (E1 against E2).** The local log-slope of an exact Yukawa potential at r = 28 is -(kappa + 1/r) = -0.4522 and of the force -0.4550. A pure exponential fitted to it over r 22 to 34 therefore returns k_E1 = 0.455 (channel 0) and 0.457 (channel 180) with the measured weights, with chi2 0.16 and 0.63 against 0 for E2 (scholar; 6 and 7 points). Measured k_E1 0.4577 and 0.4603: the value a Yukawa gives. E1's lower chi2 (2.40 against 3.32, 2.76 against 3.68; a difference of 4.6 in both channels, the reviewer's Delta AIC) is larger than exact Yukawa data with these weights give (0.2 and 0.6), so the data lean to E1 by about 4 units beyond the weights; the far-tail term of F-034 enters the integrated V_F (error bars 1% at r = 24 and 28% at r = 34, the floor of the far tail) and is the untested candidate. It is not evidence for a pure exponential: k_E1 = 0.458 is what a Yukawa returns, and k_E2 = 0.4173 +- 0.0022 equals the independently known kappa to 0.2% (0.4 standard errors).
- **N54 (the +3.4% baseline: not explained by the settle-up; corrected).** My first version took the shift of kappa alone: a shift by -9.2e-4 (0.41647 for the static profile, 0.41555 to 0.41570 for the settled pair, omega_pair 0.90950 to 0.90957, a rise of 3.5e-4 to 4.2e-4 over 0.909149) multiplies the force by exp(+9.2e-4 r): 1.026 at r = 28, 1.031 at 34, 1.037 at 40, which looked like the fitted 1.034 +- 0.004. That omitted the tail amplitude: A falls with omega (60.07 at 0.906505 and 56.70 at 0.909149 in the family, about -1275 per unit omega), so A changes by -0.8% to -0.9% and A squared by -1.6% to -1.9%, and the product is +0.4% to +1.8% over r 26 to 40 (scholar; the reviewer's +0.6% to +1.9%). Against the fitted 3.4% +- 0.4% that is a factor 2 to 5 short, so the scale is unexplained and at r 26 to 30 it carries most of the residual R. The rise of omega itself is the settle-up of a phi_t = 0 start recorded in N47(c) (+3e-4 to +5e-4 at the dip start and in Session 2). For b5 there is no such rise (pair omega 0.975594 and 0.975600 at r = 42 and 48 against 0.975598) and the tail-fit k is still 0.8% below kappa (claim 27), so a scale or a decay-constant offset of a few per cent is not tied to the settle-up in either case.
- **N55 (the candidate causes of the far-tail residual and what separates them).** (a) Coherent 3 omega interference: proportional to P (absent at the dip), independent of the box size, odd under the field flip, phase 3 dtheta, period 2.476 in r. (b) Reflection of the same waves from the sponge or box: also proportional to P and odd under the flip, but it depends on the box and the sponge; the 1.5 times box at r = 24 moved F by 2.1e-5 and the 1.33 times box rerun at r = 34, 40 moves F by 0.8% to 2.2%, at most 0.11 sigma_F (F-034): disfavoured, not excluded (no 2 times box). (c) Start transient: the window halves agree for r >= 30. (d) Estimator or centroid noise: the momentum estimator agrees; both estimators use the same fields, so a shared field-level defect is not excluded. (e) Return of slow near-gap waves from the sponge (the F-008 class): not coherent with the partner, so not odd under the flip in general. Only (a) and (b) survive the checks run so far, (b) being disfavoured by the 1.33 times box rerun, and a 2 times box would decide between them; the reviewer's status for (a) is "3 omega-type radiation interference, consistent, partly confirmed" (r >= 32, not parameter-free).

### W5. Proposed checks, not run (cheap first)

- **D-A1** (jobs like the existing r 34 to 40 pairs): dtheta = 60 degrees at r = 34, 36, 40 (and 120 degrees if one more is affordable). The 3 omega term flips against its dtheta = 0 sign (3 dtheta = 180 degrees) while the Yukawa term stays positive; this separates the phase 3 dtheta from the fundamental's phase, which 0 and 180 cannot.
- **D-A2**: a dip pair at r = 34 to 40, and at r = 22 to 28 for V_N. Predicted: no residual (5.5e-8 at r = 40), no V_N floor. Separates radiation (direct or reflected) from an analysis artefact.
- **D-A3**: a step-0.5 grid at r = 34 to 40, one channel, T and window as the production: 5 points per period 2.476; fixes k, removes the alias and gives the phase offset. A 2 times box rerun at r = 34 and 40 decides direct against reflected; the 1.33 times box rerun disfavours reflected (F-034).
- **D-A4** (calculation, no PDE): the lump's 3 omega scattering phase from the settled lump's effective potential, against delta = 0.50 +- 0.09 rad; and the sinc form (N51) for E_cloud from the stored single-lump fields (the 3 omega energy inside the box at the window), against the V_N offset at r = 20.
- **D-A5**: a second unequal pair with a different Delta omega (the orchestrator's proposal): a dressing scales with Delta omega^2 in the acceleration and a force does not; and the momentum estimator against the centroid at that pair.
- **D-A6**: V_N in two box sizes at the same d: N51 predicts the offset grows with L.

### W6. Corrections to the earlier briefs

1. **arXiv:2001.07476.** Recorded in this file (the Amin & Mou rows, about lines 221 and 883) as "Hertzberg, Schiappacasse, Yanagida", the paper on other phases cited by Amin & Mou. The abstract page ([arXiv:2001.07476](https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.07476), `ABSTRACT`, this round) is "Axion Star Nucleation in Dark Minihalos around Primordial Black Holes": no mergers, no phases. The paper on mergers and their phase dependence is [Hertzberg, Li, Schiappacasse 2020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.02405) (`ABSTRACT`: phase dependence is among the cases simulated; no finding in the abstract; merged mass about 0.7 of the sum for comparable masses). I did not re-open Amin & Mou's reference list, so which paper they meant is not settled.
2. **Bowcock, Foster, Sutcliffe** is "Q-balls, Integrability and Duality", arXiv:0809.3895 (v2, 8 January 2009), J. Phys. A 42, 085403 (2009) (the journal reference is Siemonsen & East's [76], `READ`); earlier briefs say 2008, the arXiv date.
3. **N40 and the V_N floor.** N40's illustrative F_rad at r = 40, 2.8e-4 for P = 0.03, used a power from the published range; Test B's P = 0.0122 gives 1.1e-4, the size seen. N40's warning that the step-2 grid aliases the period 2.48 into about 10.4 was right (claim 19, N50). N40 left the sign open, and the fit gives it a phase offset of 0.5 rad. N40 did not consider the cloud interference energy over the box (N51), which is what limits V_N, not the local coupling.
4. **N34** (Session 3): predicted an attractive mean force from the quartic cross term; the unequal pair's mean force is repulsive (claim 26). The secular second-order term, whose sign N34 left open, or the sextic cross term, must be larger.
5. **U6, "radiation-mediated interference force between oscillons":** the wording rule stands ("analogous to ... in optics; no source found; do not write first"); the evidence for the effect is consistent and partly confirmed (the reviewer's status; claims 19, 20), and the checks in W5 are what remains.
6. **My first N54** (the kappa shift explains the +3.4%) omitted the change of the tail amplitude; corrected above (N54, claim 17) after the reviewer's check.
