## Session 5 brief, Track C (2026-11-10, before any Track C run)

Scope: WP-A, Track C half, of `SESSION_05_PLAN.md` (sections 3 and 5, items 1 to 3), the Track C predictions C1 to C6 and the orchestrator's O1 to O7 in `SESSION_05_PREDICTIONS.md`. Model claude-sonnet-5-5; a reasoning-effort value of 30 is visible (CLAUDE.md lists xhigh for `scholar`: mismatch, noted again). Only `docs/` was written; no git commands, no code, no simulations. The Track F half is written by another worker in `S5_TRACKF_BRIEF.md`. The Sandusky, Page, Schmidt setup is in `S5_SPS1992_PARAMS.md` (the paper is paywalled; abstract only).

Tags as at the head of this file: `READ`, `ABSTRACT`, `SECONDHAND`, `DERIVED`, "no source found". How read: the fetch tool saved the PDF of every paper it could not convert and I extracted the text locally with a small PDFKit program (scratchpad); `READ` means I read the passage in that text. Two routes differ and are marked where used: (i) an abstract fetched from a publisher page through the fetch tool, which returns a paraphrase with short exact quotes (`ABSTRACT`); (ii) Sanchez-Rey et al. and Weinstein through the ar5iv HTML via the fetch tool's answer (the first, `READ` also from the PDF; the second, answer only). The 2008 Flach and Gorbach Physics Reports review (116 pages, [PDF](https://pcs.ibs.re.kr/~sflach/publications.DIR/2008/physrep_467_1_2008.pdf)) is larger than the fetch tool's 10 MB limit and was not read; only its abstract ([Bath portal](https://researchportal.bath.ac.uk/en/publications/discrete-breathers-advances-in-theory-and-applications/), `ABSTRACT`). Search numbers continue from 277; this brief uses 278 to 361.

### 1. Headline findings

1. **The standard reason breathers are said not to exist in continuous media does not cover a medium with a highest note, and nothing published answers whether C-smooth holds a trapped vibration.** Three independent sources give the mechanism as the unbounded linear spectrum of a continuous field: higher harmonics of the breather always resonate with it ([Flach and Willis, sec. XIX](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004), `READ`; [Flach and Kladko, sec. I](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709049), `READ`; [Chong and Kevrekidis, sec. 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15837), `READ`). The Segur-Kruskal, Kichenassamy, Denzler and Birnir-McKean-Weinstein results are for Klein-Gordon and sine-Gordon equations, second order in x, with a mass gap and an unbounded spectrum. C-smooth has a spectrum bounded by 1, so those results are outside its class and their mechanism is absent (`DERIVED` inference from the `READ` statements). I searched for a published breather in a continuous medium with a bounded spectrum and found none (searches 317 to 321, 345, 354, 361). The nearest are breathers in media with spatially periodic coefficients (a grid in the coefficients) and compact breathers in strictly nonlinear chains that have no linear band. So the C6 premise "published existence results rely on a grid" is right but incomplete, and the literature's own diagnosis is a bounded spectrum, which a grid supplies but is not the only way to get. The C-smooth runs are a clean test of that diagnosis.
2. **C-smooth is the locally nonlinear Eringen nonlocal elastic rod, and also the improved Boussinesq (Love rod) equation.** With the stress given by a kernel (the Green function of 1 minus d^2/dx^2) acting on the local stress F(u_x), the equation of motion becomes (1 - d^2/dx^2) u_tt = d/dx [F(u_x)], which is the plan's C-smooth ([Duruk, Erkip, Erbay 2009](https://research.sabanciuniv.edu/id/eprint/8722/1/IMA_Duruk-Erbay-Erkip.pdf), eqs. 2.1 to 2.3, `READ`; the paper's second-order choice L = 1 - gamma d^2/dx^2 with gamma = a^2/12 matches the chain's dispersion to O(a^4)). That paper proves local well-posedness in H^s with s > 1/2 for g in C^k(R) and global existence when the strain energy is non negative, and the conserved energy contains the mixed kinetic term (U_xt)^2 in displacement variables U. It does not cover a pole law like G1 (F defined only for abs(u_x) < 1); no source found for that. A better smooth candidate exists in the same paper's family: the fourth-order (HBq) form has a finite-wavenumber band maximum with zero group velocity, like the chain (section 4.6).
3. **The two patterns' stability ordering is potential and lattice dependent, and the plan's "moves" rule may misread a known behaviour.** For symmetric hardening chains (G2) four independent sources agree: bond-centred (Page, the S-bend) stable, site-centred (Sievers-Takeno, the single bump) unstable with a real Floquet multiplier (section 3.2). The ordering reverses for on-site nonlinearities (discrete nonlinear Schroedinger, some Klein-Gordon chains), for dark breathers in soft even potentials, and is spoiled by a cubic term. Flach and Gorbach report that a Sievers-Takeno breather perturbed along its unstable eigenvector then oscillates about the stable Page mode, which is centred half a site from the Sievers-Takeno site (my reading of that sentence); Duran et al. and the Sandusky abstract describe travelling motion. The plan's rule for "unstable and moves" (centroid leaves its site by at least one grid unit) would call a half-site hop "not moving". Also: loss against speed for moving breathers has resonance peaks (Duran et al.), so the C5 rule "loss per unit distance does not decrease from one speed to the next" has no support.
4. **No source found for any breather result with a log law or a FENE spring (item 3), and none for the pole law's well-posedness.** Hard-wall (vibro-impact) limits appear for Hertzian chains. The only published relatives of G1 as a continuum are the strain-limiting elasticity models of Rajagopal, studied for travelling waves, not breathers.

### 2. Conventions and dictionary

Plan G2: F(d) = d + beta d^3, energy d^2/2 + beta d^4/4. Literature "beta-FPU": H = sum [p^2/2 + (1/2)(dq)^2 + (beta/4)(dq)^4] ([Duran et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.09167) eq. 1; [Cretegny et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709204), beta = 0.1; both `READ`). Chong and Kevrekidis write F(y) = K2 y + K3 y^2 + K4 y^3, so K4 = beta ([arXiv:2408.15837](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15837), sec. 2). Sanchez-Rey et al. write V = u^2/2 + K3 u^3/3 + K4 u^4/4 ([arXiv:nlin/0306025](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), `READ`). Band: omega_q = 2 sqrt(phi_2) sin(q/2), maximum 2 for phi_2 = 1 = the plan's ceiling 2 ([Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026), eqs. 6, 7, `READ`). Plan G1: F = d/(1 - d^2) = d + d^3 + d^5 + ..., K3 = 0, K4 = 1: at small amplitude it is G2 with beta = 1 (`DERIVED`). Page = bond-centred = antisymmetric displacement = the plan's S-bend (pattern -1, 1). Sievers-Takeno = site-centred = symmetric displacement = the plan's single bump (pattern -1/2, 1, -1/2). Words "odd" and "even" are used in both directions in the literature; use the patterns (see `S5_SPS1992_PARAMS.md`).

### 3. Item 1: intrinsic localized modes, discrete breathers

#### 3.1 Existence and the role of the grid

- **Definition and condition.** A discrete breather is a time-periodic, spatially localized solution; with Fourier series in time the far tail is linear and the non-resonance condition is k Omega_b not equal to omega_q for all integers k, which for a lattice with a bounded band means Omega_b above the band maximum ([Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026), eqs. 8 to 12 and the sentence after, `READ`). The review says the same in general terms: breathers are generic and structurally stable because the non-resonance condition (the breather frequency and all its multiples must avoid the linear spectrum) is easily met on a lattice, and it is easily met because the linear spectrum of a Hamiltonian lattice is bounded ([Flach and Willis, sec. XIX A](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004), `READ`).
- **Why a grid helps, as the literature states it.** Flach and Kladko: stationary breathers are non-generic in space-continuous models because the phonon spectrum of a continuous system is typically unbounded from above; the resulting infinity of high-frequency resonances generally prevents space-localized time-periodic solutions; discreteness of space gives a shortest wavelength and hence a finite upper bound of the phonon spectrum ([arXiv:cond-mat/9709049](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709049), sec. I, `READ`). Chong and Kevrekidis (2024): coupling of higher harmonics to the continuous spectrum is generally unavoidable in a continuous system because the linear passband extends to infinity; sine-Gordon escapes by integrability; the passbands of spatially discrete systems are bounded from above, so the coupling can be avoided ([arXiv:2408.15837](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15837), sec. 3, `READ`). Flach and Willis, sec. I C: sine-Gordon breathers are isolated (Birnir, Denzler), phi^4 breathers do not exist (Segur and Kruskal; Kichenassamy), because the harmonics k omega "resonate with the linear spectrum" of the Klein-Gordon equation for large enough k (`READ`).
- **Proofs.** MacKay and Aubry (Nonlinearity 7, 1623, 1994): existence of breathers for time-reversible or Hamiltonian networks of weakly coupled oscillators ([abstract](https://wrap.warwick.ac.uk/20153/), `ABSTRACT`); the proof covers anharmonic on-site potentials with weak coupling ([Sanchez-Rey et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), sec. I, `READ`), not directly an acoustic chain like G1 or G2. For Fermi-Pasta-Ulam chains: Aubry et al. prove existence of breathers of arbitrarily small amplitude when V is even (variational method); James (C. R. Acad. Sci. 2001) proves existence, and non-existence when violated, of small-amplitude breathers just above the band if the local hardening condition B > 0 holds, B = (1/2) V''''(0) - (V'''(0))^2 for V''(0) = 1, which is B = 3 K4 - 4 K3^2 for the cubic-plus-quartic potential ([Sanchez-Rey et al., sec. I, II](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), `READ` for their statements, `SECONDHAND` for the proofs of Aubry et al. and of James; the review of Flach and Willis they cite is Phys. Rep. 295, 181 (1998); the same coefficient 3 K2 K4 - 4 K3^2 appears in [Chong and Kevrekidis, sec. 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15837), `READ`). G2 has B = 3 beta and G1 has B = 3 (`DERIVED`), so both are inside the proved class at small amplitude. Large amplitude is outside the proofs; numerics find large-amplitude breathers ([Sanchez-Rey et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), abstract and sec. III, `READ`).
- **Band-edge origin.** Breathers bifurcate from the zone-boundary plane wave (tangent bifurcation); for the symmetric case phi_(2m+1) = 0 a bifurcation exists if the plane-wave frequency is repelled upward from the band by amplitude, which holds for hardening laws; the bifurcation energy E_c scales as 1/N^2 for the acoustic chain ([Flach and Willis, sec. X, eq. 10.19](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004), `READ`). Small amplitude: omega_b approx 2 + (B/8) A^2 ([Sanchez-Rey et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), `READ`).
- **Tighter is faster, in the lattice.** Frequency detuning from the band edge scales as A^z with z = 2 for a quartic law; the spatial decay exponent delta scales as A^(z/2); and abs(Omega_b - Omega_E) is of order kappa delta^2 ([Flach, Kladko, MacKay 1997](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9701003), p. 3 to 6, `READ`). So along a lattice family the localization length falls as the frequency above the band edge rises: the ceiling signature the plan's section 2.2 rule looks for.
- **Existence range.** For beta-FPU, exact breathers exist at every frequency above the phonon band (numerics, beta = 0.1; [Cretegny et al., sec. IV](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709204), `READ`). Frequencies up to about 3.5 against a band maximum of 2 were reached by the chaotic breathers of that paper (same section). Nothing found for a finite upper limit in G2. For G1 the upper limit of the family in frequency is not found: see section 5.
- **Energy.** In one dimension breathers of arbitrarily small energy exist near the band edge: E_b of order A_0^((4 - z d)/2), which tends to 0 for d = 1, z = 2 ([FKM 1997](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9701003), eq. 6, `READ`). Large-amplitude breathers of a class of asymmetric potentials (B < 0) have an energy threshold in one dimension ([Sanchez-Rey et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), abstract, `READ`).

#### 3.2 The two patterns and their stability

Table of stability results, with the parameters they belong to. All `READ` unless marked.

| Source | Potential and parameters | Bond-centred (Page, S-bend) | Site-centred (Sievers-Takeno, single bump) |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026) | phi_2 = phi_4 = 1, phi_3 = 0, Omega_b = 4.5, 80 sites, open boundaries | stable; all Floquet multipliers on the unit circle; the abstract says it is linearly stable for symmetric FPU potentials | unstable; a localized eigenvector with a real multiplier off the circle; perturbed along it, it goes on to oscillate about the stable Page mode |
| [Duran et al. 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.09167) | beta-FPU, beta = 1, N = 60, periodic | linearly stable | unstable; a real multiplier mu > 1; perturbing along it sets the breather in motion |
| [Cretegny et al. 1998](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709204), sec. IV | beta-FPU, beta = 0.1 | stable; exact breathers stay unchanged in very long runs | unstable |
| [Sanchez-Rey et al. 2004](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), sec. II | K3 = 0, K4 = 1 (B = 3), omega_b between 2 and 2.5 | stable | a harmonic instability: one pair of real eigenvalues sigma and 1/sigma close to 1, growing with the breather frequency |
| same, K3 not zero | K3 = -0.3 and -0.6, K4 = 1, omega_b = 2.1 | oscillatory instabilities that grow with abs(K3), mostly size dependent | keeps the harmonic instability and acquires the oscillatory ones |
| [Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026) | phi_2 = phi_4 = 1, phi_3 = 0.5 to 1, Omega_b = 4.5, 80 to 640 sites | unstable, oscillatory, abs(lambda) - 1 of order 0.01 to 0.03, independent of size; becomes stable again for Omega_b above 14 at phi_3 = 0.5; once pushed along the oscillatory instability past a threshold it leaves its site and moves | keeps its strong localized instability and adds the oscillatory ones |
| [Sanchez-Rey et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), sec. IV and conclusions | dark breathers (most oscillators excited, inside the band), soft even potential K3 = 0, K4 = -1, and K3 = -0.6 | unstable | unstable at low enough frequency, linearly stable for an even potential near the band edge (ordering reversed) |
| [Johansson and Jason 2014](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.0994), sec. 1 | discrete nonlinear Schroedinger, cubic on-site nonlinearity | inter-site: unstable | on-site: stable (ordering reversed) |
| [Flach and Willis, sec. VIII](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004) quoting Campbell and Peyrard | Klein-Gordon chain, some parameter ranges | two-site solution: linearly unstable | one-site solution: stable |

What this says for C3 (G1): the ordering is not a property of "site against bond" alone. It depends on whether the nonlinearity sits on the sites or the bonds, on the symmetry of the potential (the cubic term is what spoils the Page mode: Flach and Gorbach attribute the Page-mode instabilities solely to the asymmetric term phi_3, since for phi_3 = 0 the Floquet problem has period T_b/2 and the condition omega_q + 2 Omega_b = omega_q cannot hold when Omega_b is above the band), and on the sign of the hardening. G1 is on the bonds, symmetric and hardening, so the literature lines it up with G2 at small amplitude. For large stretch near the pole: no source found.

#### 3.3 Motion: barrier, mobility, radiation while moving

- **Observed motion in one-dimensional chains.** Moving breathers were reported in 1D FPU chains by Takeno and co-workers, localized, moving over long distances without significant radiation or change of shape, with internal frequencies above the band and velocities below the maximum group velocity; Sandusky, Page and Schmidt related dynamically unstable breathers to moving ones in a careful numerical study; the practical criterion in these papers for no radiation was that the object did not radiate more than about 1% to 5% of its energy during the run ([Flach and Willis, sec. IX A](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004), `READ`). The same review: small-amplitude, weakly localized breathers move over longer distances than large-amplitude ones; in Klein-Gordon chains large-amplitude objects radiate and stop; the two-dimensional FPU lattice does not produce strongly localized moving objects; and, in 1997, the existence of exactly moving breathers was still an open question (sec. IX E).
- **Exact moving breathers in beta-FPU exist but carry wings.** Solutions that repeat after a shift by r sites in s periods, V1 = r/s sites per period, are found by Newton iteration seeded from the Sievers-Takeno breather with the momentum part of its unstable eigenvector ([Duran et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.09167), sec. 3, `READ`). For generic potentials lacking a certain symmetry, moving breathers are not spatially localized but carry oscillatory wings whose amplitude depends on the internal frequency and the velocity (sec. 1). At beta = 1, N = 60, V1 = 1/3 the wing energy rises sharply at resonant frequencies (omega approx 2.126, 2.237, 2.342 against the formula's 2.129, 2.244, 2.352), the normalized wing energy axis of their fig. 2(b) running from 1 down to 1e-10 (`READ`, figure axis labels only), and total energy is multivalued in frequency there (sec. 4); the example V1 = 14/23 at omega = 2.5 is spectrally stable, the V1 = 1/3 top branch near 2.237 has a real multiplier mu > 1 (mu = 1.0989 at omega = 2.424). Perturbed along the unstable eigenmode, the breather gradually decelerates and ends nearly at rest with its velocity oscillating about zero (periodic boundary conditions; the wings re-enter). Aubry and Cretegny: the exact mobile breather solutions they found numerically have infinitely extended tails, so the motion is non-radiative only when the breather moves in equilibrium with a particular phonon field ([arXiv:cond-mat/9712046](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9712046), abstract, `ABSTRACT`). Cretegny et al.: exact mobile breathers are dressed by a small-amplitude phonon tail, in equilibrium with emitted and absorbed radiation; chaotic breathers in the beta = 0.1 chain moved at v about 0.2 sites per unit time with lifetime about 1e4 time units for an initial pi-mode amplitude of 0.8, and their velocity increases slightly with their energy; the authors say pinning in the FPU chain may be too small to detect, whereas it is strong for large Klein-Gordon breathers ([sec. IV, V A](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709204), `READ`).
- **Condition on a moving tail.** For a moving breather u_n(t) = F(Omega_b t, n - V t), the k-th harmonic of the tail obeys k Omega_b + V q_k = omega(q_k) with q_k complex and Im z = V Im q_k; resonances with real q_k make the tail extended ([Flach and Kladko, sec. II](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709049), `READ`). Applied to the chain, with the Brillouin-zone-aliased wavenumbers, this is why exact motion needs special velocities or accepts wings (`DERIVED` reading, not stated in that form).
- **Peierls-Nabarro barrier.** The concept does not carry over cleanly: breathers come in continuous families with internal modes, so there is no unique minimum energy for translating a breather one site; the barrier is defined for adiabatic motion, which does not imply moving solutions at finite velocity; and a mode moving at nonzero velocity generically generates oscillations that resonate with linear waves, so it emits radiation and eventually slows or decays ([Johansson and Jason, sec. 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.0994), `READ`). Flach and Willis (sec. IX C): the stationary breathers are separated from moving ones by a movability separatrix, so a depinning energy has no meaning as a concept (`READ`). For the cubic on-site discrete nonlinear Schroedinger model the barrier grows in proportion to the nonlinearity strength, so highly localized solitons are pinned (Eilbeck 1986, in [Johansson and Jason](https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.0994), `READ`, `SECONDHAND` for Eilbeck). Sandusky, Page and Schmidt's relation omega_3 = 2 pi v for the modulation frequency of a moving object (Flach and Willis, sec. IX C, `READ`, `SECONDHAND` for the SPS figures).
- **A boosted, non-integrable chain has no Galilean boost.** Moving versions have to be built from the unstable eigenvector, as above, or by Newton iteration at rational velocities. In C-smooth, which is also translation invariant without a grid, a Lorentz-type boost is not a symmetry either (the equation is not Lorentz invariant). No source found for the pushed breather's loss per unit distance as a function of push strength in either medium.

#### 3.4 Stationary breather: radiation, tails, mean strain

- **No radiation for the exact periodic solution.** All harmonics sit outside the band, so the far field is evanescent; the objects seen in simulation that are quasi-periodic (a periodic breather perturbed in phase space) radiate small-amplitude waves and so are not exact solutions, and the radiation weakens as the periodic-orbit family is approached ([Flach and Willis, secs. VIII, XIX](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004), `READ`). So the C4 test must start from the periodic solution and treat a drift as the signature of a perturbed (quasi-periodic) object.
- **Tails.** The tail exponent follows from linear theory with the breather frequency as input ([Flach and Willis, sec. XIX A](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004), `READ`); in the chain the staggered deformation decays exponentially, alternating in sign ([Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026), figs. 4, 6, `READ`). For potentials made only of one anharmonic power the decay is double exponential and the profile compact-like ([Flach and Willis, eq. 4.21](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004); [Gorbach and Flach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0502063), `READ`); with a harmonic term, as in G1 and G2, the far tail is exponential. For a moving object, [Flach and Kladko](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709049) give the general tail condition above (`READ`).
- **No static far field for a symmetric potential.** A symmetric potential (no cubic term) gives the interaction a parity symmetry, so the breather contains only odd harmonics in time (A_(2k,l) = 0) and the static deformation d_+ vanishes ([Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026), section on definitions, `READ`). For an asymmetric potential the breather carries a kink-shaped static lattice deformation (their fig. 8, d_+ about -1.005 at phi_3 = 1, phi_2 = phi_4 = 1, Omega_b = 4.5).

#### 3.5 Two and three dimensions

- Breather energies have a positive lower bound if the lattice dimension d is at least the critical dimension d_c = 4/z, with z the detuning exponent (z = 2 for a quartic law, so d_c = 2; that paper says d_c is typically 2 and not greater than 2); the minimum-energy breather in 3D is strongly localized, a few lattice spacings wide; for d = 1 the energy goes to zero with amplitude, except for nonlinearity powers mu of at least 5 in a discrete nonlinear Schroedinger model ([Flach, Kladko, MacKay 1997](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9701003), `READ`; Kastner extends the scenario: d_c can generically be greater than two, and there are breather systems that do not come from a band-edge bifurcation, [arXiv:nlin/0304058](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0304058), abstract and intro, `READ`). Weinstein proves thresholds for ground-state breathers of discrete nonlinear Schroedinger-type systems exactly when sigma is at least 2/d ([arXiv:patt-sol/9903001](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9903001), fetch answer from ar5iv, `READ` by that route).
- Mobility: the 2D FPU lattice does not produce strongly localized moving objects ([Flach and Willis, sec. IX A](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004), `READ`).
- Not tested in Session 5 (Track C is one-dimensional). Relevant to the Session 6 parking-lot item on a ceiling medium in three dimensions.

### 4. Item 2: the grid question

#### 4.1 Non-existence results in continuous media: what each covers

| Source | Equation class | Result | Tag | C-smooth inside the class? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Segur and Kruskal, PRL 58, 747 (1987)](https://www.osti.gov/biblio/6837533) | phi^4 Klein-Gordon in 1+1 dimensions, amplitude and frequency just below the uniform-oscillation frequency | no true breathers in that limit; approximate breathers radiate beyond all orders of the expansion | `ABSTRACT` | no: second order in x, mass gap, unbounded spectrum; C-smooth has a mixed term u_xxtt and no gap |
| [Kichenassamy, CPAM 44, 789 (1991)](https://arxiv.org/abs/1709.07787) | nonlinear Klein-Gordon equations, formal power series in the amplitude under a sign condition | the series is pole free in the unit disc only if the equation is essentially equivalent to sine-Gordon | `ABSTRACT` | no, same |
| Birnir, McKean, Weinstein, CPAM 47, 1043 (1994), "The rigidity of sine-Gordon breathers" | perturbations of sine-Gordon | only special perturbations keep breathers (the summaries name sin u, u cos u and a third form); no full text seen | `SECONDHAND` (descriptions in a 1995 Denzler reference list and in citing papers, search 306) | no |
| [Denzler, CMP 158, 397 (1993)](https://web.math.utk.edu/~jdenzler/Publications/abs-brea-cmp.text) | u_tt - u_xx + sin u = eps Delta(u), Delta analytic near 0 | most of the sine-Gordon breather family cannot persist at first order, one-dimensional exceptional space | `ABSTRACT` | no |
| Flach and Willis 1997; Flach and Kladko 1997; Chong and Kevrekidis 2024 | general statement of the mechanism | unbounded linear spectrum, so harmonics resonate | `READ` (quoted in 3.1) | mechanism absent: the C-smooth spectrum is bounded by 1 (`DERIVED`) |

Answer to "is C-smooth inside the class": no. All four theorems are for second-order-in-x Klein-Gordon-type equations with a continuum of linear frequencies reaching infinity. C-smooth has omega^2 = k^2/(1 + k^2), below 1 for all k, and every harmonic k Omega of a state with Omega above 1 lies above it. None of those results speaks to a bounded-spectrum continuum. Neither does any other source I found, in either direction.

#### 4.2 Existence results in continuous media

- **Spatial periodicity.** Blank, Chirilus-Bruckner, Lescarret, Schneider (CMP, 2011) prove real, time-periodic, exponentially localized breathers for a nonlinear Klein-Gordon equation s(x) u_tt - u_xx + q(x) u = u^3 with spatially periodic step-function coefficients (`SECONDHAND`, from the descriptions in [arXiv:2407.10766](https://arxiv.org/html/2407.10766v1) and a Karlsruhe lecture, search 310; the paper itself not seen). Hirsch and Reichel extend it to periodically extended delta potentials (`SECONDHAND`, search 310). Of the breather-type solutions of such a heterogeneous model the 2024 paper reports "breather solutions are generically unstable" (`SECONDHAND`, search 317). These media have a grid in their coefficients.
- **No linear spectrum.** Rosenau and Schochet (PRL 94, 045503, 2005): certain strictly nonlinear one-dimensional lattices support discrete breathers over a large localized region that becomes exactly compact in the continuum limit; tails decay double exponentially ([abstract](https://cris.tau.ac.il/en/publications/almost-compact-breathers-in-anharmonic-lattices-near-the-continuu/), `ABSTRACT`; companion Chaos 15, 2005, via [Gorbach and Flach](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0502063), `READ` for the description). The chains are purely anharmonic, with no linear dispersion; the width of the linear spectrum is zero. This is a medium with no long waves that travel, not a ceiling medium. Compactons (Rosenau and Hyman, PRL 70, 564, 1993): solitary waves with compact support in KdV-like equations with nonlinear dispersion ([search 356](https://www.osti.gov/biblio/7179523), `SECONDHAND`); they travel.
- **Continuum, bounded spectrum.** Searches for a breather, standing wave or time-periodic solution of the improved Boussinesq equation, an Eringen-type nonlocal rod, a Rosenau-regularized chain or a double-dispersion equation returned nothing (searches 317, 345, 354, 361): no source found.

#### 4.3 The candidates

| Candidate | Equation | Linear band | Ceiling | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Inertia with a built-in length (Love rod, improved Boussinesq, regularized long wave, microinertia) | u_tt - u_xxtt = d/dx [F(u_x)] = C-smooth | omega^2 = k^2/(1 + k^2) | 1, approached only as k goes to infinity; group velocity (1 + k^2)^(-3/2), `DERIVED` | Love rod: the dispersion follows trivially from the equation (`DERIVED`; context in the Rayleigh-Love literature, `SECONDHAND`, search 358); quasi-continuum of dense lattices: Rosenau 1986 and 1988 ([Oruc, Borluk, Muslu](https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.03928), sec. 1, `READ`; [Duruk et al.](https://research.sabanciuniv.edu/id/eprint/8722/1/IMA_Duruk-Erbay-Erkip.pdf), sec. 2.4, `READ`) |
| Nonlocal elasticity, Eringen differential type, locally nonlinear stress | stress = kernel convolved with F(u_x), kernel the Green function of 1 - gamma d^2/dx^2 | omega^2 = c^2 k^2/L(ik) with L(ik) = 1 + gamma k^2 | 1/sqrt(gamma); polynomial approximations of L(ik) imply an upper bound for omega | [Duruk et al. 2009](https://research.sabanciuniv.edu/id/eprint/8722/1/IMA_Duruk-Erbay-Erkip.pdf), eqs. 2.2 to 2.4 and sec. 2.3, `READ`. This is the same equation as the row above (`DERIVED`: (L u)_tt = (F(u_x))_x with L = 1 - d^2/dx^2). |
| Strain-gradient elasticity (stiffness gradient only) | u_tt = u_xx - l^2 u_xxxx type | omega^2 = k^2 (1 + l^2 k^2), unbounded | none | `DERIVED` (the sign that is well posed); no source read |
| Gradient elasticity with both inertia and stiffness gradients | | omega^2 = c^2 k^2 (1 + l_s^2 k^2)/(1 + l_i^2 k^2), bounded by l_s^2/l_i^2 when l_i is larger | finite | [Metrikine and Askes 2002](https://scispace.com/papers/one-dimensional-dynamically-consistent-gradient-elasticity-2oz7jbyldy): a later paper cites it for avoiding high-frequency waves when the inertia length exceeds the stiffness length (`SECONDHAND`, search 357); formula `DERIVED` |
| Strain-limiting elasticity (Rajagopal), with stress or strain gradients | strain bounded, stress unbounded | KdV long-wave limit; dispersive once gradients are added | not stated | [Erbay and Sengul, ZAMP 76, 94 (2025)](https://orca.cardiff.ac.uk/176956) (`ABSTRACT` via search 336): smooth solitary waves; [arXiv:1507.07344](https://arxiv.org/abs/1507.07344) (`ABSTRACT`): travelling waves in the viscoelastic version |

So "nonlocal elasticity" and "inertia with a built-in length" are the same medium in one dimension, and strain-gradient elasticity alone has no ceiling. The medium the plan calls C-smooth is the standard continuum model of a dense chain with a highest note, in three independent guises.

#### 4.4 Is the improved Boussinesq equation well posed for a pole law, and what are its localized solutions

- **Well-posedness, what is known.** [Duruk, Erkip, Erbay 2009](https://research.sabanciuniv.edu/id/eprint/8722/1/IMA_Duruk-Erbay-Erkip.pdf), `READ`: Cauchy problem for u_tt - u_xx - u_xxtt + beta u_xxxxtt = (g(u))_xx. Theorem 2: for s above 1/2, g in C^k(R) with g(0) = 0, local well-posedness in H^s by contraction mapping, with the maximal time characterized by blow-up of the L-infinity norm of u (eq. 3.11). Theorem 4: global solutions for s at least 1 if g is in C^(s+1)(R) and the integral G of g is non negative (Remark 4: bounded below by minus k u^2 suffices). The global existence of the generalized improved Boussinesq equation (beta = 0) was proved by Chen and Wang (Nonlinear Anal. 36, 961, 1999; cited there, not read). The conserved energy is ||Lambda^(-1) u_t||^2 + ||u||^2 + ||u_t||^2 + beta ||u_xt||^2 + 2 integral G(u) dx, in the strain variable u = U_x; in the displacement variable the kinetic part is U_t^2 + U_xt^2 (+ beta U_xxt^2). [Oruc, Borluk, Muslu](https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.03928), eq. 2.3, `READ`: I_3 = integral [U_t^2 + 2 F(U_x) + U_x^2 + eta_1 U_xt^2 + eta_2 U_xxt^2] dx. Blow-up in finite time for focusing powers is shown numerically and by a criterion (their theorem 6.1, from the Duruk-Erbay-Erkip class).
- **Pole law G1.** Not covered: the lemmas and theorems need g smooth on all of R; F(d) = d/(1 - d^2) is defined only for abs(d) < 1. No source found for the improved Boussinesq equation or any nonlocal elastic rod with a pole law. `DERIVED`: the equation is an ordinary differential equation in a Banach space, w_tt = -F(w) + K * F(w) with K the smoothing kernel (1/2) e^(-abs(x)) (the same structure as the proof of Theorem 2), so a local solution exists as long as sup abs(w) stays below 1 and the energy bound controls the integral of -(1/2) ln(1 - w^2) but not the maximum of abs(w); a pointwise approach of abs(w) to 1 in finite time is therefore not excluded by the energy. The plan's guard is the right device.
- **Localized solutions that are known.** Travelling solitary waves. For the quadratic or cubic nonlinearities the sech-type solutions have speed c with c^2 above 1 (HBq: u = A sech^gamma(B(x - c t - x_0)), the exponent gamma fixed by the nonlinearity power p and garbled in the text extraction, with c^2 > 1; [Oruc, Borluk, Muslu](https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.03928), eqs. 2.4 to 2.8, `READ`); the improved Boussinesq equation "is not fully integrable" and their head-on collisions are inelastic with secondary waves. `DERIVED` for G1: with w(x - c t), integrating twice gives c^2 w'' = c^2 w - F(w); the homoclinic orbit exists when 1 - w_*^2 = exp(-c^2 w_*^2) has a root in (0, 1), which it has for every c above 1; the amplitude w_* tends to 1 as c grows. These are supersonic, travelling, and not trapped vibrations. Compactons and peakons: found for Boussinesq-like equations with nonlinear dispersion, a different class (search 361, `ABSTRACT`-level hits); no source found for the linear-dispersion improved Boussinesq. Anything standing (a breather, a time-periodic localized solution): no source found.

#### 4.5 What is and is not a trap in C-smooth (`DERIVED`, for the reviewer)

- The C-smooth linear band has no maximum: omega rises monotonically to 1 and the group velocity is nonzero for every finite k. There is no standing zone-boundary mode, so the band-edge bifurcation route to a breather that the lattice theory uses ([Flach and Willis, sec. X](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004)) has no direct analogue; the envelope route would need a carrier at k going to infinity.
- A plane wave of wavenumber k_0 and amplitude a in the hardening law G2 has omega^2 = k_0^2 (1 + (3/4) beta a^2)/(1 + k_0^2) (from w_tt - w_xxtt = (w + beta w^3)_xx with the cubic term at first harmonic). It exceeds the ceiling 1 once (3/8) beta a^2 is larger than about 1/(2 k_0^2). So "rate above 1" alone is not a trap in C-smooth: extended waves of large wavenumber have it too. The plan's trapped test (twin comparison, localization) is the right discriminator.
- The corner and narrow-pluck content of the linear twin is held by the linear medium (O3, O6): the group velocity (1 + k^2)^(-3/2) goes to zero.

#### 4.6 A better smooth candidate: the fourth-order (HBq, bi-Helmholtz) medium

Proposal, `DERIVED` from the `READ` equations of [Duruk et al. 2009](https://research.sabanciuniv.edu/id/eprint/8722/1/IMA_Duruk-Erbay-Erkip.pdf) (eq. 1.1, 2.9, theorem 1) and [Oruc, Borluk, Muslu](https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.03928) (eq. 1.3, 2.3). In the plan's units (long-wave speed 1, length unit chosen so the second-order inertia coefficient is 1):

- Equation of motion: (1 - d^2/dx^2 + beta d^4/dx^4) u_tt = d/dx [F(u_x)], beta > 0. For the stretch w = u_x: w_tt - w_xxtt + beta w_xxxxtt = (F(w))_xx.
- Linear band: omega^2 = k^2/(1 + k^2 + beta k^4). It has a maximum at k_m = beta^(-1/4), omega_max = (1 + 2 sqrt(beta))^(-1/2), with zero group velocity, and tends to 0 as k goes to infinity. The maximum is a genuine standing band edge at finite wavenumber, as in the chain, but the medium is continuous and translation invariant. Choosing beta = 3/5 matches the chain's dispersion to O(k^6) (the paper's gamma_1 = a^2/12, gamma_2 = a^4/240 give beta = gamma_2/gamma_1^2 = 144/240); then k_m = 1.136 and omega_max = 0.626 in these units (`DERIVED` arithmetic).
- Energy: E = integral [ (1/2) u_t^2 + (1/2) u_xt^2 + (beta/2) u_xxt^2 + W(u_x) ] dx, with W' = F, W(d) = -(1/2) ln(1 - d^2) for G1 and d^2/2 + beta_s d^4/4 for G2. (Consistent with the `READ` conserved integrals I_3 and the energy identity in Duruk et al.; it includes every term in the equation of motion, standard 3.)
- Why better: it has the lattice's band structure (a finite-k maximum), no grid, a proven Cauchy theory for smooth laws, and every harmonic k Omega above omega_max is non-resonant. Why not: waves with k above k_m have reversed group velocity and the band falls to 0 at large k, so very short waves are low notes; the "ceiling" is a maximum of the band, not an upper limit on the notes of every wave of every scale in the sense Aaron describes. It is a comparator, not a replacement.
- Considered and not recommended: the "shift-operator" medium u_tt(x) = F(u(x+1) - u(x)) - F(u(x) - u(x-1)) with x real. It has omega = 2 sin(k/2) for all real k and no fixed points, but it splits into independent chains indexed by the fractional part of x, so it is the grid again.
- Status: a suggestion for the orchestrator, outside the plan's scope guard; put it in the report's parking lot unless Track C finishes early. No source found for breathers of this medium either.

### 5. Item 3: springs with a limit of stretch

- **FENE springs, logarithmic potentials, Born-Infeld-type chains.** Three searches on FENE (329, 333, 353) and two on logarithmic, Born-Infeld and hard-wall chains (330, 360): no breather result for any of them. The FENE hits are polymer rheology; the FPU solitary-wave hits (nonconvex FPU chains, Hertzian chains) are not FENE. No source found.
- **Hard-wall limits of strongly nonlinear chains.** James (2018): travelling breathers and solitary waves of the discrete p-Schroedinger equation for Hertzian-type (power-law, compression only) chains, with an analytical approximation in a "vibro-impact limit" of large p ([Phil. Trans. R. Soc. A 376, 20170138](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6077854), `ABSTRACT` via search 359). The force law is a power of the compression, not a pole in tension, but the large-p limit is a hard wall, the limit G1 approaches as the stretch goes to 1 (`DERIVED` 5.1 below).
- **Toda contrast.** No breathers in the Toda chain, numerically (searches for discrete breathers in a Toda lattice were fruitless) and by the plane-wave criterion: for phi_2 = 1, phi_3 = -1/2, phi_4 = 1/6 the inequality 3 phi_2 phi_4 >= 4 phi_3^2 fails (1/2 against 1) ([Flach and Willis, sec. X C](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004), `READ`; the arithmetic `DERIVED`). Toda is integrable; no moving-breather conclusion drawn.
- **Lennard-Jones, Morse, Born-Mayer.** Direct numerical searches found no intrinsic localized modes for these potentials, and the authors conclude they do not exist in monatomic lattices with them ([Sandusky and Page 1994](https://journals.aps.org/prb/abstract/10.1103/PhysRevB.50.866), `ABSTRACT`). The cubic term is large in these potentials, so B = 3 K4 - 4 K3^2 is negative for the small-amplitude theory; large-amplitude breathers with an energy threshold exist for a class of such asymmetric potentials ([Sanchez-Rey et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/nlin/0306025), `READ`). G1 is the other way round: symmetric, hardening at all orders.
- **A limit of stretch in a continuum.** Rajagopal's strain-limiting elasticity has strain bounded and stress unbounded, the continuum counterpart of G1. Published work studies travelling waves and KdV limits ([Erbay and Sengul](https://orca.cardiff.ac.uk/176956), `ABSTRACT`); no breather result.
- **What happens as the stretch approaches the pole (`DERIVED`, 5.1).** For G1 the zone-boundary plane wave obeys a_tt = -4a/(1 - 4a^2) (stretch s = 2a): a one-degree-of-freedom oscillator with potential -(1/2) ln(1 - 4a^2). Its period is T = 2 integral_0^(s_m) ds / sqrt(ln((1 - s^2)/(1 - s_m^2))), frequency 2 at small amplitude, rising without bound as s_m goes to 1, to leading order like pi sqrt(ln(1/(1 - s_m^2))). The energy per site goes to infinity logarithmically. So a G1 breather family can in principle be followed to any frequency while its peak stretch stays below 1; there is no finite upper edge of the window in frequency, only an edge in stretch. The window in length and rate is then set by stability and by the trapped test, not by existence (this bears on Aaron's "narrow range" expectation; see section 10).

XIX A](https://arxiv.org/abs/patt-sol/9704004); [Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026) figs. 4, 6; [Flach and Kladko](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9709049) eq. 6, analytic continuation of the dispersion relation; all `READ`). The lattice formula 2 cosh(kappa/2) = Omega follows from omega = 2 sin(q/2) at q = pi + i kappa; the C-smooth kappa = omega/sqrt(omega^2 - 1) from q = i kappa in omega^2 = q^2/(1 + q^2); both `DERIVED`. Caveat from the sources: a harmonic's forced nonlinear tail can decay more slowly than its free one, and for pure power potentials the tail is double exponential (the far tail with a harmonic term is exponential). |
| O2 surroundings | known for the lattice; `DERIVED` for C-smooth | For a symmetric potential the breather has only odd harmonics and zero static deformation, A_(2k,l) = 0 and d_+ = 0 ([Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026), sec. "some definitions", `READ`). Odd harmonics only means u(t + T/2) = -u(t), so the time average of displacement and stretch is zero (`DERIVED`). Asymmetric potentials give a static kink-shaped deformation (same paper, fig. 8). For C-smooth the same symmetry argument holds for F odd (`DERIVED`). Applies to the exact periodic object, not to a perturbed one. |

The retained-energy formula I re-derived and it agrees: energy spectrum proportional to k^2 exp(-k^2 sigma^2) for u = A exp(-x^2/(2 sigma^2)), group velocity below 0.005 for k above 5.76, giving erfc(a) + (2 a/sqrt(pi)) exp(-a^2) with a = 5.76 sigma; at sigma = 0.1 that is 0.87 (the 0.88 quoted is within rounding) (`DERIVED`). No published statement of this holding. |
| O4 lone-oscillator rate | no source found | The formula T = 4 integral_0^a dw / sqrt(ln((1 - w^2)/(1 - a^2))) is the period of w_tt = -w/(1 - w^2) (potential -(1/2) ln(1 - w^2)); correct as algebra (`DERIVED`); the lattice analogue in 5.1. |
| O5 no selected size, winding up | no source found | Mechanism is nonlinear phase mixing: local frequency depends on position, neighbours dephase (Dawson 1959, via [secondary descriptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.02773), `SECONDHAND`, search 352). The structural fact behind it, that the improved Boussinesq equation is an ordinary differential equation in a Banach space with a smoothing coupling, is `READ` in the proof of [Duruk et al. Theorem 2](https://research.sabanciuniv.edu/id/eprint/8722/1/IMA_Duruk-Erbay-Erkip.pdf); the winding-up claim itself, and "frac(t) agrees across h while pointwise profiles do not", have no source. The coupling kernel suppresses fine-scale structure as 1/(1 + k^2), so fine scales decouple further (`DERIVED`, supports O5). |
| O6 corner stays | no source found | `DERIVED`: with w_tt = -F(w) + K * F(w) and K * F smooth, each point evolves by its own ordinary differential equation under a smooth drive, so a jump in w stays at its place; in the linear twin the high-k content of a jump has omega going to 1, so the corner oscillates at the ceiling rate. [Duruk et al. Theorem 3](https://research.sabanciuniv.edu/id/eprint/8722/1/IMA_Duruk-Erbay-Erkip.pdf) (`READ`): regularity in x is preserved but not improved. A jump in w is outside their class (s above 1/2), so the theorem does not state it. |
| O7 sigma = 0.5, peak stretch 0.8 | no source found | Low confidence as flagged. Nothing published to compare. |

### 8. The gate: should the C-smooth production runs be made, or made differently

**No: nothing I found says the C-smooth production runs should not be made.** The non-existence results that look like they forbid a trapped vibration in a continuous medium are for Klein-Gordon-type equations with an unbounded spectrum and do not apply to C-smooth; no source says a bounded-spectrum continuum cannot hold one, and none says it can. The result is open, which is what the plan says it is testing. Things to make differently or add, all advisory, none blocking:

1. State in the report that the literature expects neither outcome for C-smooth; a negative result is not "as expected" and a positive result is not "as expected".
2. The energy function must contain the mixed kinetic term: E = integral [(1/2)(u_t^2 + u_xt^2) + W(u_x)] dx (`READ`-consistent, section 4.4). Leaving out u_xt^2 is the kind of omission the lessons file warns about.
3. Keep the linear twin and the trapped test as written: in C-smooth a plane wave of large wavenumber can have rate above 1 by amplitude alone (4.5) and the narrow-pluck content of the linear twin is held by the linear medium (O3), so rate above 1 is not by itself a trap.
4. Discretization: the equation is an ordinary differential equation in a Banach space with the bounded operator (1 - d^2/dx^2)^(-1) d^2/dx^2 (Duruk et al.'s proof structure), so a spectral or high-order scheme with no stability limit from the stiff term is natural; any scheme with spacing h is itself a grid with its own ceiling, which is what the 1/4, 1/8, 1/16 test is for. Resolution has to be checked against the winding-up of O5: the scheme's cutoff pi/h will be reached by phase mixing in a time of order 1/h.
5. The |d| < 1 guard is the right device: the energy conservation does not by itself keep sup abs(w) below 1 (section 4.4), and a trip is a finding.
6. Optional comparator, scope-guard permitting: the fourth-order medium of section 4.6, which has the chain's finite-k band maximum. Parking lot unless time allows.

### 10. Conflicts with the plan's predictions and corrections to its literature column

Conflicts with published results:

1. **C2 and rule 2.3 ("moves").** The plan counts "moves" as the energy centroid leaving its start site by at least one grid unit with co-moving fraction at least 0.90. Flach and Gorbach (phi_2 = phi_4 = 1, Omega_b = 4.5) report that the Sievers-Takeno mode, perturbed along its unstable eigenvector, goes on to oscillate about the stable Page mode (whose position is half a site from the start, my reading); Duran et al. (beta = 1) and the SPS abstract report travelling. Which happens may depend on the perturbation (my inference, not stated in the sources). A start from the exact periodic Sievers-Takeno solution with noise may settle at the Page position, shift 0.5, and be scored "unstable, did not move" although the physics is the same. Suggest the rule quote the Floquet multiplier first and report the centroid track (half-site hop, travel, or neither) as it is.
2. **C5 rule, monotone loss.** The 2.3 condition "the energy lost per unit distance does not decrease from one speed to the next" has no source. The only measured loss structure (Duran et al.) is the wing energy against frequency at fixed velocity, with sharp resonance peaks and multivalued branches; a non-monotone dependence on push strength is likely. The plan's rule would then read a resonance feature as a failure.
3. **C5 basis.** The SPS "smoothly travelling version" is a different object (the abstract states a nonconstant phase difference between adjacent relative displacements) and no loss-against-speed statement is in the abstract. Replace the basis by the Flach-Willis, Duran, Cretegny and Johansson-Jason statements above.
4. **Aaron's expectation "narrow window", Question 2.** In beta-FPU, exact breathers exist at every frequency above the phonon band (Cretegny et al., beta = 0.1, numerics) and in one dimension their energy goes to zero at the band edge. The width of the window in frequency in C-grid with G2 is not narrow from existence; any narrowing has to come from stability or from the trapped test. For G1 the window in frequency has no finite upper edge (5.1). The plan's section 2.5 rule for "narrow" (less than a factor 2 in length and in rate) is not supported by existence results; keep it as a measurement, expect "not narrow".
5. **SPS growth rate 0.15.** The unit "in units of the mode frequency" is not fixed by the abstract; the multiplier per period under the most natural reading is 2.57. Do not use it as a pass line.

Corrections to the plan's literature column (`SESSION_05_PLAN.md` section 5):

- C1 basis: Sievers and Takeno (1988) is a proposal (the abstract proposes a new kind of localized mode in a pure anharmonic lattice); existence is shown by Page (1990, asymptotic), Sandusky et al. (1992, numerics), James (2001, small-amplitude proof, B > 0) and Aubry et al.; add [Flach and Gorbach 2005](https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/0410026) for a reproducible setup.
- C2: the 32,000 figure is in the SPS abstract for the even-parity mode; the abstract does not give the potential, the amplitude or the frequency of that run.
- C5: see conflict 3.
- C6: change "rely on a grid" to "rely on a bounded linear spectrum", as above.
- Parity words: SPS call the site-centred (-1/2, 1, -1/2) pattern "odd-parity"; Duran et al. call the site-centred mode the one with "even symmetry about the center". The plan's words follow SPS. Use the patterns.
- Not a conflict but a physical confirmation: along lattice families the localization length falls as the frequency above the band edge rises (FKM 1997), the ceiling signature of plan section 1.

### 11. DERIVED notes (not literature; reviewer to check before use)

N56. C-smooth equals the locally nonlinear Eringen differential rod: with S = alpha * sigma, sigma = F(u_x), alpha the Green function of 1 - gamma d^2/dx^2, rho u_tt = S_x gives (1 - gamma d^2/dx^2) u_tt = (F(u_x))_x; gamma = 1 is the plan's C-smooth; the kernel is (1/(2 sqrt(gamma))) exp(-abs(x)/sqrt(gamma)).
N57. C-smooth lattice-matching: gamma = a^2/12 matches omega = (2c/a) sin(ka/2) to O(k^4); then the ceiling is c sqrt(12)/a = 3.46 c/a against the lattice's 2 c/a. A match at the band edge would need gamma = a^2/4. The plan's "smallest scale 1" does not fix this; it is a choice to state.
N58. Plane-wave test above the ceiling (4.5): omega^2 = k_0^2 (1 + (3/4) beta a^2)/(1 + k_0^2) at first harmonic for F = w + beta w^3; exceeds 1 when (3/8) beta a^2 > 1/(2 k_0^2) at large k_0.
N59. Solitary waves of C-smooth with G1: c^2 w'' = c^2 w - F(w); homoclinic if 1 - w_*^2 = exp(-c^2 w_*^2) has a root in (0, 1); exists for c above 1.
N60. Zone-boundary plane wave in G1: a_tt = -4a/(1 - 4a^2); period and large-amplitude behaviour as in 5.1.
N61. Floquet multiplier from a rate of 0.15 per unit of angular frequency: exp(2 pi 0.15) = 2.57.
N62. Fourth-order candidate: omega_max = (1 + 2 sqrt(beta))^(-1/2) at k_m = beta^(-1/4); beta = 3/5 matches the chain to O(k^6).

Date: 2026-11-10. Author: `scholar` (Track F half). Written only in `docs/`. Nothing committed. `PRIOR_ART.md` and `SESSION_05_PREDICTIONS.md` were not touched; the orchestrator folds this file in. Search numbers are S5F-1 onward; the orchestrator renumbers.

**Evidence tags** (as at the head of `PRIOR_ART.md`, with the exact meaning used here).

- `READ`: I extracted the full text of the arXiv PDF locally (macOS PDFKit) and read the passage cited. `READ (part)` means I read the sections named and not the rest.
- `ABSTRACT`: I saw only the abstract (arXiv abstract page or search-result abstract text).
- `SECONDHAND`: another source says the paper says this, or the only access was a summary written by the web-fetch tool.
- `DERIVED`: my own algebra from the plan's equations or from numbers in a `READ` source. Not a literature claim. Re-derive before relying on it. Items are numbered DF-1 onward and listed in section 6. DF-1, DF-4, DF-5 and DF-7 were also checked numerically (a short script, scratch only; results are quoted in the items).
- "no source found": I searched (see the log) and found nothing.

Notation. S = sum over i of |d_i u|^2, X = |u_t|^2 - S (no 1/2), as in the plan. Amin's variable is X_A = -(1/2) d_mu phi d^mu phi, which in our units is X_A = X/2 (DF-2). L_O and L_R as in the plan.

---

### Gate (answer first)

**YES. The equation-R production runs may go ahead.** The plan's stop condition (section 8: "L_R is not well posed for this use, or the October lesson applies to the full form") is not met. Reasons, in three lines.

1. L_R, with the full square root, is hyperbolic, subluminal and has positive energy density for X < 1. This is Babichev, Mukhanov, Vikman (READ), Adams et al. (READ; their eq. 7 is this Lagrangian's series), Eperon, Reall, Sbierski (READ), and my own algebra (DF-1, DF-4).
2. Three of the four clauses of the October lesson are reversed for L_R (sign of the quartic term, propagation speed, energy bound). The fourth, the static no-go, still holds for L_R, but it only rules out static solutions, not oscillons (DF-6).
3. Amin's existence sign condition is satisfied: Delta = xi_2 = +1/2 > 0 for L_R (Amin eq. 24, READ; map in DF-2).

**Conditions to carry into the run set-up.** None of these stops the start.

- **G1. The guard "X < 1" is not the whole well-posedness condition for the pinch.** For the single bump (one fixed direction) X < 1 is exactly the hyperbolicity condition. For any configuration with more than one field direction active, X < 1 is necessary but not sufficient (DF-5). For the pinch u = f r-hat the extra condition is f_t^2 < 1 + f_r^2 + (f/r)^2. The radial solver cannot see its violation, because the mode that goes bad is the angular one. Add this to the WP-B2 guard and log it. Verified with an independent determinant calculation (DF-5); the reviewer should re-derive it.
- **G2. A guard trip at X -> 1 is a documented physical outcome, not necessarily a bug.** For the massless Born-Infeld/Nambu-Goto equation, a regular graph at t = 0 reaches 1 - X = 0 with a curvature singularity in finite time (Eggers and Hoppe, READ), including for radially symmetric membranes. For the equation with a mass term no existence or blow-up result was found. So log min(1 - X), max |p| (p the conjugate momentum), and where and when, and call a trip a finding with a diagnosis, as the plan's stop condition already says.
- **G3. F1 should be recorded as resting on existence only.** Amin's paper gives existence of small-amplitude k-oscillons and, in the same paper, a long-wavelength instability in three space dimensions (his d >= 3) for small amplitude. It does not give trapping or lifetimes for DBI. Pre-register: small-amplitude starts of R are expected to collapse or disperse by Amin's own criterion; a trapped state, if any, is at large amplitude, where no source was found.
- **G4. The X^2 truncation stays forbidden.** It reintroduces loss of hyperbolicity (for a static gradient with 2/3 < S < 2) and a static energy that is negative for S > 4 (DF-7).
- **G5. (Optional, cheap.)** The single bump in the three-component theory has two transverse polarizations that propagate at exactly the speed of light on top of the oscillating background (DF-5). Their parametric stability is not covered by Amin (single field). No source found.

---

### Three most important findings

1. **L_R is the DBI Lagrangian of Amin's paper with f = 1 and V = phi^2/2.** Existence sign condition Delta = 1/2 > 0 holds. But Amin's own stability result says the small-amplitude 3D family is unstable (long-wavelength, same criterion as the canonical model). I found no published stable 3D k-oscillon family. The one statement about large-amplitude DBI is "rather intriguing dynamics", with the details left to an unpublished Pearce and Amin paper (section 1.6).
2. **The October lesson does not transfer.** Its retired term is L = X_A - 4 kappa X_A^2 (xi_2 = -4 kappa < 0). L_R has xi_2 = +1/2 > 0. I rebuilt the lesson's own numbers (the -3 kappa phi_t^4 energy term and the speed sqrt((1 + 12 kappa b^2)/(1 + 4 kappa b^2))) from that Lagrangian, so the identification is exact (DF-3).
3. **Plan-relevant correction to O9 and to WP-B2 check 6.** O9's algebra is right (DF-1, to 1e-14). But "X cannot trip the guard for finite p" understates the risk (G2), and X < 1 is not sufficient for hyperbolicity of the pinch (G1).

---

### 1. Item 4: a Born-Infeld (DBI) scalar with a mass term

#### 1.1 L_R and its place in the literature

- L_R = 1 - sqrt(1 - X) - |u|^2/2 is the Dirac-Born-Infeld Lagrangian of Amin's paper with f = 1 and a quadratic potential. Amin writes it as L = f^-1(phi) [1 - sqrt(1 - 2 f(phi) X_A)] - V(phi), says it gives xi_2 = 1/2, and says he analysed large-amplitude oscillons in it with V = phi^2/2, results not presented ([Amin 2013](https://arxiv.org/abs/1303.1102), sec. 2 and 5, `READ`).
- In 1+1 dimensions, 1 - sqrt(1 - X) with X = u_t^2 - u_x^2 and no potential is the Born-Infeld scalar, the gauge-fixed Nambu-Goto string ([Eperon, Reall, Sbierski](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.03020), sec. 3.2-3.3, `READ`; their c = +1 theory).
- The same expansion appears as the textbook example of a brane fluctuation: L = -f^4 sqrt(1 - (dy)^2) = f^4(-1 + (dy)^2/2 + (dy)^4/8 + ...), which they note has the "correct" (positive) quartic sign ([Adams, Arkani-Hamed, Dubovsky, Nicolis, Rattazzi](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0602178), eq. 7, `READ`). Our L_R has the series X/2 + X^2/8 + ..., the same.

#### 1.2 Hyperbolic, positive energy, for X < 1

- **General k-essence criterion.** For L(X_A, phi) the field equation has principal part G^{mu nu} d_mu d_nu phi with G^{mu nu} = L_X g^{mu nu} + L_XX d^mu phi d^nu phi (their 2.4). It is hyperbolic (G Lorentzian) iff 1 + 2 X_A L_XX / L_X > 0 (their 2.5). The null energy condition is L_X >= 0; violating it means an energy unbounded below. They add that L_X must nowhere vanish or become infinite, since those points are singularities of the emergent geometry, and that a well-posed Cauchy problem does not guarantee a global solution ([Babichev, Mukhanov, Vikman 2007](https://arxiv.org/abs/0708.0561), sec. 2, 4, `READ (part)`).
- **No superluminality** for a single derivatively coupled scalar in a general background needs L to be convex in X: L'' >= 0 ([Adams et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0602178), p. 11, `READ`).
- **For L_R**: L_X = 1/sqrt(1 - X) > 0, L_XX > 0, and 1 + 2 X_A L_XX/L_X = 1/(1 - X) > 0 for X < 1. All three conditions hold exactly when X < 1. L_X diverges as X -> 1, which Babichev et al. flag as the singular point of the emergent geometry (`DERIVED`, DF-2, from their criteria).
- **Energy.** With p the momentum conjugate to u, H = sqrt((1 + p^2)(1 + S)) - 1 + |u|^2/2, and 1 - X = (1 + S)/(1 + p^2). So H >= |u|^2/2 >= 0, and X < 1 is equivalent to p finite (`DERIVED`, DF-1; the same as O9). The same holds for the three-component field with p a vector and S the full sum.
- **Local Cauchy problem.** Hyperbolicity gives local well-posedness by the standard quasilinear theory (Kato; Hughes, Kato, Marsden; not read, no arXiv text). The mass term is a lower-order term and does not change the principal part. I did not find a source that states this for the massive Born-Infeld scalar, so "local well-posed for X < 1" is `DERIVED` plus standard theory, not a quoted result.

#### 1.3 Propagation speeds along and across the gradient

Single field. Background u_t = a, gradient of size b along a unit vector n. Define X = a^2 - b^2. `DERIVED` (DF-4); checked numerically against the closed forms (agreement to the 7 digits printed).

| Direction of the wave vector | Speed | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| across the gradient (k perpendicular to n) | c_perp^2 = (1 - X)/(1 + b^2) = 1 - a^2/(1 + b^2) | at most 1 |
| along the gradient (k parallel to n) | v = (-a b +/- sqrt(1 - X))/(1 + b^2) | magnitude at most 1; equals 1 only at X = 0 |
| gradient-free (b = 0) | c^2 = 1 - a^2 = 1 - X | the DBI speed limit; also Amin eq. 25, c_s^2 = (1 + 2 X_A T_XX/T_X)^-1 = 1 - 4 xi_2 X_A + ..., `READ` |
| static (a = 0) | c_perp = 1, c_par = 1/sqrt(1 + b^2) | subluminal along the gradient |
| retired term, static, for comparison | c_par^2 = (1 + 12 kappa b^2)/(1 + 4 kappa b^2) | exactly the speed in LESSONS item 6; above 1 |

The characteristic cone is the null cone of the induced (Nambu-Goto) metric. Eperon et al. state the c = +1 theory is subluminal in the sense that the null cone of g lies on or inside that of the Minkowski metric (their 3.12, `READ`), consistent with the table.

Vector-valued field (the pinch, and the bump with its two extra components): see DF-5 and G1. In the three-component theory two polarizations propagate on the light cone and one on a DBI-type cone, and X < 1 does not guarantee that the DBI-type cone is real.

#### 1.4 Can X reach 1, or smoothness be lost in finite time from smooth data

Without a potential (the massless Born-Infeld equation):

- Exceptional, no shocks. In the 1+1 case a wavepacket moves without distortion and never forms a shock (the property called "exceptional"; Taniuti 1959 via [Eperon et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.03020) `READ`). For the Born-Infeld electrodynamics the same word means no shocks form, and "completely exceptional" is Boillat's term; a 3D result of Christodoulou is reported for the scalar (Chaplygin) case ([Kadlecova](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.12249), intro, `SECONDHAND`, which cites Boillat and Christodoulou, neither read).
- Simple waves do not steepen. Both the canonical and the DBI scalar are free of caustics for planar simple waves, where generic k-essence is not ([Mukohyama, Namba, Watanabe 2016](https://arxiv.org/abs/1605.06418), `ABSTRACT`; against [Babichev 2016](https://arxiv.org/abs/1602.00735), `ABSTRACT`, who argued generic k-essence forms caustics).
- But smooth data can still fail in finite time by another route. In the 1+1 massless equation z_tt(1 + z_x^2) - z_xx(1 - z_t^2) = 2 z_t z_x z_xt, "can develop singularities in finite time, starting from finite initial data". The singularity is a swallowtail: the graph is regular at t = 0 and has a curvature singularity at t = 1 in their explicit example (their eqs. 31 and 32), and for t > 1 is no longer a graph. At the singular point L^2 = 1 - z_t^2 + z_x^2 = 0, which in our variables is X = 1 ([Eggers, Hoppe 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0812.1790), `READ`). They state the same singular solution applies to radially symmetric membranes and to higher dimensions (their sec. III).
- Eperon et al. describe the same thing as two strong wavepackets meeting and the string folding back, so Phi would have to become multivalued; for small amplitude the packets pass through each other with a time delay, for large amplitude a singularity forms, and the maximal globally hyperbolic development is then not unique in the subluminal theory ([Eperon et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/1802.03020), sec. 1 and 3.4, `READ (part)`).
- Small data are fine: global existence for small data of the timelike minimal-surface (Born-Infeld) equation in Minkowski space ([Lindblad 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0210056), `ABSTRACT`). Small 1+1 solutions decay inside a smaller region of the light cone ([Alejo, Munoz 2017](https://arxiv.org/abs/1707.02595), `ABSTRACT`). Explicit self-similar blow-up solutions of the 1+1 equation, with a nonlinear stability result ([Yan 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.04227), `ABSTRACT`).

With a potential:

- For Born-Infeld scalars with a potential that multiplies the square root, V(T) sqrt(1 + (dT)^2), and runaway V, caustics (multivalued regions) form generically, in 1+1 and in 3+1 ([Felder, Kofman, Starobinsky 2002](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0208019), `ABSTRACT` plus the first two sections read). That is a different structure from L_R (an additive mass term, no runaway), so it is context, not a prediction for us.
- For the DBI scalar plus an additive quadratic potential, as in L_R: **no source found** on well-posedness, global existence or blow-up (searches S5F-7, S5F-18).

What follows for the runs. The singularity mechanism is amplitude-driven (strong, colliding packets; X -> 1 needs |u_t| of order the Born-Infeld speed, which in our units is field amplitude of order 1/omega). An oscillon at large amplitude is a standing superposition of left- and right-moving pieces, so G2 is a live possibility in the large-amplitude part of the family. Not a prediction; no source applies.

#### 1.5 Amin's existence condition ([arXiv:1303.1102](https://arxiv.org/abs/1303.1102), PRD 87, 123505, `READ`)

- Lagrangian L = T(X_A, phi) - V(phi), X_A = -(1/2) eta^{ab} d_a phi d_b phi (mostly plus signature, so X_A = (1/2)(phi_t^2 - |grad phi|^2)), T = X_A + xi_2 X_A^2 + xi_3 phi X_A^2 + ..., V = phi^2/2 + lambda_3 phi^3/3 + lambda_4 phi^4/4 + ... (eqs. 1 to 3).
- Small-amplitude expansion phi = eps phi_1 + ..., tau = sqrt(1 - eps^2) t, rho = eps r. The profile equation is f'' + ((d - 1)/rho) f' - f + (3/4) Delta f^3 = 0 (eq. 18) with **Delta = xi_2 - lambda_4 + (10/9) lambda_3^2** (eq. 19). Oscillons exist iff Delta > 0 (eq. 24, boxed). For a quadratic potential, xi_2 > 0 suffices, which he states is equivalent to c_s < 1 (eq. 25).
- 1D: f = sqrt(8/(3 Delta)) sech(rho). 3D: numerical, f_0 = 3.06699 sqrt(8/(3 Delta)) (eq. 32). No simulations tables for non-canonical models are given.

**Map to L_R** (`DERIVED`, DF-2): X_A = X/2, T = 1 - sqrt(1 - 2 X_A) = X_A + X_A^2/2 + X_A^3/2 + (5/8) X_A^4 + ..., so xi_2 = 1/2, lambda_3 = lambda_4 = 0, m = 1. **Delta = 1/2 > 0: the sign condition is satisfied.** At leading order in 3D (using S(0) = 4.33738768 and the integral of S^2 = 18.897 for the canonical 3D profile, from Fodor et al., `READ` in the Session 1 brief): phi(0) = 7.08 eps and E = 25.2/eps + O(eps), with omega^2 = 1 - eps^2. So the amplitude goes to zero and the energy diverges as eps goes to zero. Context: valid only where the single-frequency small-amplitude expansion is, eps below about 0.25 in the canonical case.

For the single bump u = f e in the three-component theory, S = f_r^2 and |u_t|^2 = f_t^2, so L_R reduces exactly to the single-field DBI Lagrangian. Amin's single-field result applies to that invariant sector.

#### 1.6 What Amin found for stability and lifetime, and later work

- **Stability criterion** (eqs. 53 to 56): small-amplitude oscillons are stable against long-wavelength perturbations iff dN/d eps > 0, with N = eps^{2-d} times the integral of f^2 d^d rho. So stable for d = 1, 2 and **unstable for d >= 3**, "confirmed by numerical simulations" with no data shown. The typeset eq. 56 ends in "proportional to eps^{d-2}", which contradicts the first part of the same line and eq. 54; the text and the first equality give eps^{2-d}. Treat that as a typo.
- He says large amplitudes may remove the 3D instability (as in Amin and Shirokoff, where a large sextic term does it), and that "no general stability condition exists" for large amplitude. The sextic term is what turns N(eps) around there ([Amin, Shirokoff 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.3380), `READ` in the Session 1 brief).
- **Lifetime**: no number. The radiating tail is expected to be exponentially small, decay rate of order exp(-1/eps), citing Segur-Kruskal and Fodor et al. He computes radiation only in an expanding background (1+1).
- **DBI at large amplitude**: "rather intriguing dynamics appearing at large amplitudes", reference [53] Pearce and Amin "in progress"; acknowledgment thanks M. Pearce for simulations of large-amplitude DBI oscillons (sec. 5 and 6, `READ`). **I found no published Pearce and Amin paper** (S5F-1, S5F-9, S5F-16).
- Later work found:
  - [Sang, Huang 2021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.14697) (PLB 823, 136781), 3+1D lattice, DBI kinetic term, axion-monodromy potential, formation during preheating. Reports oscillon-like overdensities with space-averaged Lorentz factor up to about 3.9 and says formation is suppressed by the non-canonical term; gives no lifetimes, amplitudes or frequencies. `SECONDHAND` (fetch-tool summary of the ar5iv text; the PDF exceeds the tool's size limit).
  - [Sakstein, Trodden 2018](https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.07724) (PRD 98, 123512): oscillon-type quasi-breathers sustained by the quartic galileon, only C^1. `ABSTRACT`. Different structure.
  - [van Dissel, Sfakianakis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.07789) (PRD 106, 096018) say non-canonical oscillons have been considered only for single fields (their ref. 8 is Amin) (sec. II, `READ (part)`).
  - [Diaz-Alonso, Rubiera-Garcia 2007](https://arxiv.org/abs/0705.0112) (PLB 653, 445): some k-field models with kinetic self-coupling do have static, finite-energy, spherically symmetric solutions, under conditions on the model (vanishing vacuum energy, positive-definite energy density). `ABSTRACT`. This does not contradict DF-6 (L_R is not shown to be in that class).
  - [Endlich et al. 2010](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.4873) (JHEP 05 (2011) 73): no stable static solitons in a broad class of derivatively coupled theories including some k-essence models. `ABSTRACT`.
- No source found for any measured lifetime of a DBI-plus-mass oscillon in 3+1 (S5F-1, S5F-9, S5F-11, S5F-16).

#### 1.7 Energy minimum, stable branch, saturating term (3D)

- Canonical sextic case: the family has an energy and N minimum at finite amplitude, which is the edge of the stable branch (details and numbers in `PRIOR_ART.md` 2.2 to 2.4, `READ`). Examples: [Fodor et al. 2008](https://arxiv.org/abs/0802.3525), E = 6.29908/eps + 264.262 eps for the phi^4 double well; [Amin, Shirokoff](https://arxiv.org/abs/1002.3380), N minimum near alpha^2 = 0.025; [Li, Yamaguchi, Zhang 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13276), beta = 4/3, omega_death = 0.97, N = 80.3. All from the Session 1 brief; not re-opened this round.
- k-oscillon case: the small-amplitude end gives N proportional to 1/eps and E = 25.2/eps for L_R (section 1.5), so E rises without bound toward small amplitude. Whether E turns up again at large amplitude, i.e. whether the kinetic nonlinearity supplies its own saturation, **no source found**. Amin says large amplitude "requires a case by case analysis" and gives none for DBI. The expansion has no small parameter here (Delta = 1/2 is order one, unlike the large-g limit of Amin and Shirokoff), so the interior minimum, if it exists, sits at order-one amplitude where only numerics can decide. This is why F1 and F2 for R rest on the runs, not on theory.

### 2. Item 5: does the October lesson apply to the full square-root L_R

**Answer: No.** Three clauses are reversed; the fourth (static no-go) holds but does not constrain oscillons.

The retired term. LESSONS item 6 gives W = phi'^2/2 + 3 kappa phi'^4 - V, a term -3 kappa phi_t^4 in H, and a speed sqrt((1 + 12 kappa phi'^2)/(1 + 4 kappa phi'^2)). I rebuilt all three from L = X_A - 4 kappa X_A^2 - V (DF-3). So the retired term is xi_2 = -4 kappa < 0 in Amin's notation.

| Clause | Retired term (LESSONS item 6) | L_R, full square root | Source, tag |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) Static regular solution | none exists | **none exists either** | `DERIVED` DF-6: the quantity H_l = V - (1 - 1/sqrt(1 + phi'^2)) satisfies dH_l/dr = (2/r) phi'^2/sqrt(1 + phi'^2) >= 0 with H_l(0) = V(phi_0) >= 0 and H_l(infinity) = 0, so phi_0 = 0. Derrick bracket is also a sum of non-negative terms. Endlich et al. (`ABSTRACT`) are consistent. **Harmless for oscillons**: Amin's k-oscillons are time-periodic and need no static balance (`READ`). |
| (b) Sign of the quartic terms | +kappa grad^4 in the static energy (beats Derrick); -3 kappa phi_t^4 in H | xi_2 = +1/2: static gradient energy S/2 - S^2/8 + ... (the quartic is negative, so Derrick is made worse, not better); H in velocity variables = u_t^2/2 + (3/8) u_t^4 + ... (positive). In momentum variables the quartic is -p^4/8, but sqrt(1 + p^2) - 1 is non-negative, so the sign of a truncated coefficient is not the criterion | `DERIVED` DF-2, DF-3; sign is the "correct" one in [Adams et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/hep-th/0602178) eq. 7 (`READ`); Amin: xi_2 > 0 gives Delta > 0 (`READ`) |
| (c) Propagation speed | superluminal along gradients | subluminal everywhere in X < 1; at most 1 (table in 1.3) | Adams et al. p. 11 (L'' >= 0, `READ`); Eperon et al. (3.12) (`READ`); DF-4 |
| (d) Energy bounded below | no (-3 kappa phi_t^4) | yes: H >= |u|^2/2 >= 0 | `DERIVED` DF-1; NEC L_X > 0 ([Babichev et al.](https://arxiv.org/abs/0708.0561), `READ`) |

**O9, checked against sources.** The conjugate-momentum form H = sqrt((1 + p^2)(1 + S)) - 1 + |u|^2/2 and the statement "the X^2 term has the opposite sign to the retired one" are both confirmed (DF-1: Legendre transform checked numerically, maximum error 7e-15 over 10,000 random states; DF-3). No source contradicts them. Two cautions, from G1 and G2: p can diverge in finite time in the massless limit, so "X stays below 1" is not a reason to drop the guard; and for the three-component field X < 1 does not give hyperbolicity.

**What truncating at X^2 would do** (`DERIVED`, DF-7). L_T = X/2 + X^2/8 - |u|^2/2.

- For a static gradient S, hyperbolicity needs (1 - 3S/2)/(1 - S/2) > 0, which fails for 2/3 < S < 2 (the equation changes type) and for S > 2 the coefficient of the kinetic term is negative (the null energy condition is lost).
- Static energy density S/2 - S^2/8 is negative for S > 4: unbounded below.
- So the truncated model has both pathologies of the retired term, at a gradient |grad u| of order one in units of the limit. Amin uses the X^2 truncation legitimately, only as a leading-order small-amplitude expansion (`READ`). The plan is right to forbid it for production.
